Some provisions (1.4 (partially), 2.2 (partially), 4.1, 6.4, 8.1 (partially), 9.2, 9.6 and 9.7, Section B (B-2 to B-6) and Section D Appendix D) are excluded from AVV and are only binding for organized employers and their employees. The AVV takes effect the day after publication in the Staatscourant or on the date specified in the decision, and runs until the CAO's end date at the latest. The trade in building materials is defined as an enterprise or department primarily engaged in the wholesale of building materials and related articles, where building materials turnover is at least one-third (1/3) of total trade turnover. The salary of the adult employee is determined once a year on March 1, taking into account the number of years of experience the employee has been classified in a job group, until the maximum number of job years in the respective group has been reached. Those who have reached the highest level in their salary scale only receive the general salary increases per CAO."],

    ["The employer can apply a performance-related pay scheme (Afdeling D, Bijlage A). The purpose of this scheme is to provide an assessment of the employee's performance in their current role. The assessment determines the employee's salary growth within their salary scale. Additionally, the scheme aims to explore and record the employee's development opportunities in their current role or a potential future role with the current or another employer. If no scheme agreed upon with the Works Council or employee participation is yet present in the organization, every employee annually receives a standard salary increase of 1 periodical until the maximum of the scale corresponding to the function performed by the employee is reached. Annually, the employee - if they have actually been employed for 12 months in the same function - gains an additional year of service. The employee becomes entitled to the next periodical in their salary scale. Mandatory frameworks for such a scheme include three discussions (planning, interim evaluation, performance review), covering objectives and development goals, and three assessment levels (Below expectation, As expected, Above expectation). The assessment result is linked to remuneration: 'Below expectation' means no periodical (conditional on an improvement plan), 'As expected' means 1 periodical, 'Above expectation' means 2 periodicals. An 'Under expectation' assessment can only be given if, prior to the assessment interview, at least one discussion has taken place in which the 'Under expectation' performance has already been discussed and agreements have been made about an improvement and/or training trajectory. This trajectory must be facilitated by the employer, agreements must be recorded in writing, and the employee must be given reasonable time to demonstrate improvement. All assessments and agreements must be recorded in writing. The Works Council/employee participation must be consulted and give consent for the scheme's introduction. An objection procedure allows employees to appeal assessments to their manager and, if unresolved, to the Vaste Commissie, which reviews the procedure's thoroughness, application, and substantiation."],

In addition, the rule applies that a young person stays in their step for a maximum of 1 year, so after 1 year they move to the next step. This means for a 17-year-old in group 3: a starting salary of €1386/month; after 1 year (measured from the date of employment) as an 18-year-old he receives €1733/month; after 2 years (measured from the date of employment) as a 19-year-old he receives the 'professionally adult' starting salary (step 0) that belongs to employees aged 20 and older.       "Scope of agreement (by nature of work, Article 3.1): Applies to enterprises that wholly or partly: Exploit dredging equipment; Undertake and/or execute dredging or ancillary works and engage in associated wet transport; Undertake and/or execute activities on wet disposal sites; Undertake and/or execute dredging works for cable and pipeline trenches, dredging, suction, spraying, moving, and extracting materials for industrial or other purposes (including wet sand extraction); Perform activities in the gravel dredging industry; Engage in the construction, improvement, and maintenance of water defenses, water, rail, road, or pedestrian paths, harbors, earthworks, and sites for industry, recreation, and civil and utility construction."

    [
      "Scope of agreement (composite enterprises, Article 3.3): For composite enterprises that, in addition to water construction/dredging, are also active as a construction company or contracting company, this CAO applies if water construction/dredging activities predominate. Predominant activities are determined by comparing remunerated amounts or by more than 50% of total hours within the enterprise being spent on the activities mentioned in Article 3.1."
    ],
    [
      "Scope of agreement (separate department, Article 3.4): If the same situation as described in Article 3.3 occurs in a separate department of an enterprise, the same scope as in Article 3.3 applies to this separate department."
    ],

      "Dispensation from scope (Article 4.1): If the employer wishes dispensation from the application of Article 3, he must submit a written request to the Paritaire Commissie Waterbouw mentioned in Article 7."
    ],
    [
      "Collegial lending/hiring of employees (Article 5): Employer is authorized to lend out an employee for work within the Netherlands and the Dutch continental shelf for equipment rental or participation in a combination. In other cases, only with employee's consent. Employer must stipulate that the hirer fully applies this CAO. Lending employer remains liable for CAO application. Employment relationship with lending employer is maintained; hiring employer cannot terminate individual employment contract. Legal regulations for hiring/lending remain fully applicable."
    ],
    [
      "Disputes Committee (Article 6): All disputes arising from or in connection with this CAO and its implementation are submitted to a disputes committee. This includes disputes between: a. an employer and an employee bound by this CAO concerning their employment contract; b. employee organizations and an individual employer bound by this CAO; c. the Vereniging van Waterbouwers and employee organizations concerning CAO interpretation. Disputes under c. are handled to the exclusion of the ordinary court. Disputes regarding compliance also fall under the committee's competence. Investigations into suspected violations are conducted by the Secretariat of CAO Parties, which reports to the disputes committee."
    ],
    [
      "Paritaire Commissie Waterbouw (Article 7): Established at Sir Winston Churchillaan 299 A, 2288 DC Rijswijk. Handles: dispensation requests (Article 4); disputes on technical necessity of a five-day workweek (Article 13.1); crew strength regulation (Article 16.4); special cases (Articles 14 and/or 15); requests (Articles 37.5 and 37.6); exemption applications for nationality requirement for captain of seagoing water construction (Appendix 6). Provides advice to the Ministry of SZW and/or UWV on requests for reduced working hours permits. Criteria for dispensation (Article 7.5): a. employer's situation is (temporarily) so deviating that full CAO application cannot reasonably be expected; b. a deviating employment conditions package has been established between sufficiently representative and independent parties (e.g., Temporary Employment Conditions for deployment on trailing suction hopper dredgers for the Dutch coast on the Dutch Continental Shelf, Appendix 7). Requests must be submitted in writing or digitally to info@waterbouwers.nl, stating name/address of applicant, signature, precise description of request (including CAO provision(s)), facts/arguments, motivated proposal for applicable employment conditions, and date. Requests must include work description, duration, equipment, and crew strength list. Timely submission: four weeks, or two weeks in unforeseen circumstances before work starts, or up to one week after reason for change arises for ongoing work/new phase. Decision within two weeks, extendable once by two weeks. Decision communicated in writing with reasons."
    ],
    [
      "Appendix 6: Exemption Scheme for Nationality Requirement for Captain of Seagoing Water Construction. This regulation allows for dispensation from the nationality requirement for captains on seagoing water construction equipment under the Dutch flag. The Paritaire Commissie Waterbouw grants permission for the employment of captains from aspirant EU countries (Bulgaria, Estonia, Croatia, Latvia, Lithuania, Poland, Romania) and 'third countries' (Australia, Canada, People's Republic of China, Philippines, Hong Kong, India, Indonesia, New Zealand, Ukraine, Pakistan, Russian Federation, Singapore, Vietnam, South Africa) with whom the Netherlands has recognition agreements. The scheme applies to captains on seagoing water construction equipment under the Dutch flag, not to equipment without its own propulsion. The goal is to regulate the labor market for Dutch and other EU/EER captains. Companies must apply in writing to the Paritaire Commissie Waterbouw. The committee assesses if optimal use of Dutch captains has been made. Decisions require unanimity. Permission is for three years, extendable. Criteria: captain meets training requirements; no forced dismissal of Dutch/EU/EER captains; company cooperates with employment research. Procedure suspended if complaints from first mates (Dutch/EU/EER nationality) about not being appointed captain are received. Complaints can be submitted to the Geschillencommissie Waterbouw."
    ],
    [
      "Appendix 7: Temporary Employment Conditions for Deployment on Trailing Suction Hopper Dredgers on the Dutch Continental Shelf. This agreement is between Vereniging van Waterbouwers, Nautilus International, and CNV. It applies from the day an employee works on a trailing suction hopper dredger on the Dutch Continental Shelf. 'Abroad' refers to working under this dispensation scheme on the Dutch Continental Plat, including the Westerschelde and excluding the Waddenzee. 'Employee' is a Dutch resident employed under Dutch law on a trailing suction hopper dredger, who would fall under the CAO Waterbouw. 'Net foreign wage' is the net wage paid, including holiday allowance, based on an 84-hour workweek, also granted during leave. 'Gross foreign wage' is the derived gross wage for administrative/fiscal processing. 'Life partner' is spouse, registered partner, or cohabiting partner with a notarized cohabitation agreement. 'Deployment declaration' is a notification to the employee stating specific arrangements (not conflicting with these conditions), including place/project, function, duration, and assurance against displacement due to internationalization of fleet personnel."
    ],
    [
      "Appendix 8: Protocol Agreements for Sustainable Employability and Labor Market & Education. CAO parties commit to investing in sustainable employment relationships and employability. A constructive and innovative business climate fosters sustainable employability, requiring involved employers, managers, and employees. Active dialogue between employer, unions, and works councils is crucial. Focus on development in current and future functions. This will be elaborated through: 1. development of a future-oriented agenda by parties; 2. anchoring in Broad Consultation; 3. anchoring in local consultation (e.g., works councils). Regarding RVU scheme after 2025: Principle agreement to extend indefinitely, provided fiscal provision remains and costs stay equal. If costs rise, parties will consult. Paritaire Commissie Waterbouw assesses if extra €300 payment is due in compelling cases. Regarding Labor Market & Education: Joint policy for attracting, binding, and retaining qualified personnel. Increased efforts for inflow of young people (O&O-fonds Waterbouw with an information officer). More specific agreements with schools on water construction education, flexibility, and sufficient internship places. Attention to inflow of temporary workers and flexible workers. Agreement on retaining employment (personnel exchange)."

    [
      "Basic weekly wage (Article 1.6): The gross basic hourly wage multiplied by the number of hours of the normal workweek (36 hours) as per Article 12. Includes all reimbursements for commuting time and travel time at work, excluding work-to-work travel and Article 21. If applicable, increased by a personal guarantee allowance. Wage tables per function group are in Appendix 1. All amounts are paid according to fiscal regulations."
    ],
    [
      "Basic hourly wage (Article 1.7): The amount set for the function in the wage table (see Appendix 1)."
    ],
    [
      "Service allowance (Article 18.2): Each employee is paid a service allowance of 7.3% of the basic weekly wage."
    ],
    [
      "Holiday allowance (Article 18.3): The holiday allowance is 8% and is paid periodically or in May. It is calculated over the reference wage (Article 1.13), excluding the holiday allowance."
    ],
    [
      "Wage payment (Article 19.1): Wages are paid weekly on payday, observing Articles 19.2 to 19.4. This applies even if an employee is dismissed or resigns on a day other than payday."
    ],
    [
      "Pendagen (Article 19.2): The employer may introduce one to five 'pendagen' (days between end of work period and payday). If more than two are introduced, the employer must provide an advance on the weekly payday equal to at least the net basic weekly wage due for days worked. This advance cannot be deducted from subsequent payments, but will be settled with the final wage upon termination."
    ],
    [
      "Shifting wage payment (Article 19.3): The employer may shift payment of a portion of a week's wages to the next payday, provided at least the basic weekly wage due for days worked is paid on the normal payday of that week."
    ],
    [
      "Monthly/four-weekly payment (Article 19.4): The employer may calculate wages over a period of one month or four weeks and pay the basic weekly wage and service allowance no later than the week following that period. Other remuneration components must be paid no later than one month or four weeks following that period. If switching from weekly to monthly/four-weekly payment, a three-month transitional arrangement must be implemented to ensure employees gradually adapt."
    ],
    [
      "Wage specification (Article 19.5): A written or electronic specification must be provided with wage payment (Art. 7:626 BW), including: a. Gross wage (basic weekly wage, service allowance, overtime, watch allowances, other allowances/surcharges); b. Employer/employee name, period calculated, agreed working hours, whether indefinite employment contract is in writing; c. Deductions (payroll tax, (company) pension, other industry-specific schemes, employee's social security premium share); d. Accrual of holiday allowance and days; e. Applicable minimum hourly wage based on age."
    ],
    [
      "Structural wage increases (Article 20.1): Wages are increased by: 4% as of February 1, 2025; 4% as of February 1, 2026; 1% as of February 1, 2027. Changes are implemented in the first full wage week after the change date, unless it falls on a Monday (Article 20.2)."
    ],
    [
      "Structural year-end bonus (Article 20.3): Set at 4% of the gross annual wage in 2025 and 2026, excluding holiday allowance. Paid no later than December 31. Paid pro rata if employment is terminated during the calendar year."

      "Remuneration in special situations (Article 21): 'Working in special situations' means serving on equipment laid up at a storage facility, staying at a shipyard for repairs, or shut down (for reasons other than frost), or in a comparable situation. Employees in these situations receive the basic weekly wage and associated service allowance (Article 21.2). Additionally, if applicable, travel expenses, food allowance, watch allowance, and equipment allowance are paid (Article 21.3). Employees performing such work are allowed to travel weekly during working hours, or the determined travel time is reimbursed at the basic weekly wage (Article 21.4). If an employee travels daily instead of weekly at the employer's request, daily travel during working hours is allowed, or travel time is reimbursed at basic weekly wage with a deduction of one travel hour per day (Article 21.5). Travel time is calculated at an average speed of 60 km/hour along the most common route. This calculation also applies if the employee arrives on Sunday evening and stays overnight in a hotel (Article 21.6)."

Employees have the right to inspect the system but are not provided with it. This provision does not apply if the employer has obtained a legal exemption for paying the statutory minimum wage."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:2 General Salariëring: Salary scales are functional and entry-level. Entry-level scales will be abolished on January 1, 2013. Amounts apply to full-time employment; pro-rata for part-time. For employees with an annual working time exceeding 1878 hours, salary is adjusted by the factor (contract hours / 1878). Salary for trainees is determined according to Chapter 5."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:4 Application of Salary Scales (until January 1, 2013): For functional scales 5-40, 1 function year is required; for 45-60, 2 function years; for 65-80, 3 function years. The employer can agree to fewer required function years. If an employee does not have the required function years, the corresponding entry-level scale applies, starting at 0 function years or higher based on experience. The employer can apply the entry-level scale longer if job performance warrants it, with written motivation."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:4 Application of Salary Scales (from January 1, 2013): Employees are placed at the 0 function years amount of their functional scale, or higher based on experience. Employees in an entry-level scale on December 31, 2012, will be horizontally transferred to their functional scale on January 1, 2013, to the next higher insertion number if their current number is not available. From January 1, 2013, employees aged 23 and older placed below the minimum wage will be placed at the next insertion number above the minimum wage within the scale after a maximum of one year."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:6 Year-end Bonus (until January 1, 2012): Employees in service on December 31 of a calendar year receive a year-end bonus of 6.25% of the actual earned salary (Article 1:1d) from January 1 to December 31 of that year. This is paid in December 2011."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:6 Year-end Bonus (from January 1, 2012): Employees in service on December 31 of a calendar year receive a year-end bonus. In 2012, it is 6.50% of the actual earned salary (Article 1:1d) from January 1 to December 31. In 2013, it is 6.75%. If employment ends before December 31, a pro-rata year-end bonus is paid. An employee is entitled to a year-end bonus only once per actual earned salary in a calendar year."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:8 Periodieke Verhogingen (Periodic Increases): Unless otherwise specified in the employment contract, a salary increase within the functional scale is granted once a year. The first periodic increase is granted 1 year after hiring or promotion to a higher job group. The employer can decide to grant no increase or multiple increases in a year based on a periodic systematic appraisal system that is institution-wide, transparent, offers appeal possibilities, and allows for discussion to prevent future negative appraisals."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:9 Bijzondere Toeslagen (Special Allowances): The employer can grant a gratuity. A temporary allowance of up to 10% of the applicable salary can be granted in special cases for specific functions experiencing a labor market shortage. For special circumstances in job performance, an allowance can be granted such that the sum of salary and allowance does not exceed the maximum of the next higher functional salary scale, and the allowance does not exceed 8 consecutive insertion numbers from the employee's current salary."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:10 Vakantietoeslag (Holiday Allowance): The holiday allowance is 8% of the actual earned salary from June 1 to May 31 for employees with a full year of service on May 31. It is calculated at least on the basis of insertion number 8, pro-rata for part-time employment. It is paid once a year in May. Pro-rata calculations apply for employees with less than a full year of service or whose employment ends before May 31. For fixed-term employees, the provisions regarding payment in May and pro-rata calculations are not mandatory."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:11 Bevordering (Promotion): Upon promotion to a function classified in a higher job group, the salary is taken from the functional scale of the new function, with the current salary increased by 2 periodic increments. The new salary must be at least the minimum of the new functional scale but not exceed its maximum. Until January 1, 2013, promotion to the entry-level scale of the higher job group is possible if the employee was previously in an entry-level scale. The employer can deviate from this with the works council's consent by establishing its own regulation."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:12 Waarneming (Acting Allowance): An employee designated to act in a higher-classified function (not for vacation replacement) for more than 1 month continuously receives a monthly allowance equal to the difference between their current salary and the starting salary of the higher function, with a minimum of 2 periodic increments. The total (salary + allowance) cannot exceed the maximum of the higher function's scale. If acting for at least half of the daily working hours, the allowance is pro-rata. The employer can deviate from this with the works council's consent by establishing its own regulation."
    ],
    [
      "Article 4:13 Gratificatie Jubileum en OBU (Jubilee Gratuity and Bridging Benefit): An employee with uninterrupted service receives a one-time gratuity of 1/4 monthly salary at 12.5 years, 1/2 monthly salary at 25 years, and a full monthly salary at 40 years. Upon termination due to reaching AOW-eligible age, the employee receives 1/2 monthly salary. Upon termination due to using the bridging benefit from Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn, the employee receives 1/2 monthly salary (paid if no new employment, or at the end of a new contract if one is entered into). For 'ruil-OBU', the gratuity is paid when full OBU starts and no new employment is entered into. If multiple gratuity rights overlap, only the AOW-related gratuity applies."
    ],
    [
      "Article 5:3 Salarisschaal Leerlingen (Trainee Salary Scale): For trainees with a trainee employment contract following qualification level 2, 3, 4, or 5, the salary is as per the table (not provided in text), based on a full-time working week of 1878 hours per year. Trainees have the right to interrupt work for in-school learning (max 160 hours/year) with salary retention, pro-rata for part-time. For qualification level 5 trainees, deviations are possible for predominantly theoretical second years with internships. Higher salaries can be agreed for trainees aged 21 or older if they have a relevant diploma, there's a regional labor market shortage, or they are re-entrants, provided the employer establishes a regulation with the OR/PVT. This age limit can be deviated from for trainees under 21 with a relevant diploma. During the vocational preparatory period for nursing/care trainees, a gross pocket money equal to the minimum (youth) wage is granted, pro-rata for part-time, unless they receive an equal or higher benefit."
    ],
    [
      "Article 5:7 Stagiairs (Interns): Interns are entitled to an internship allowance if the internship is mandatory, lasts longer than one month, and has a minimum duration of 150 hours. The allowance is 275 EUR gross per month (as of August 1, 2009) for an average of 36 hours per week, including expenses. This allowance increases to 300.00 EUR gross per month from September 1, 2011, 305.00 EUR from September 1, 2012, and 310.00 EUR from September 1, 2013. The allowance is pro-rata for stages of less than 36 hours per week. This does not apply to social internships."
    ],
    [
      "Article 5:9 Assistent-geneeskundigen in opleiding tot Arts voor Verstandelijk Gehandicapten (Assistant Doctors in Training for Doctors for the Intellectually Disabled): Until January 1, 2013, the entry-level scale of the lowest functional scale for doctors applies. From January 1, 2013, the lowest functional scale for doctors applies. Salary determination is based on contractually agreed working hours. They have the right to interrupt work for in-school learning (return day, minimum 160 hours/year) with salary retention, pro-rata for part-timers. From February 1, 2012, full-time employees have this right for in-school learning on the return day, pro-rata for part-timers."
    ],
    [
      "Article 5:10 Gezondheidszorgpsycholoog in opleiding (Healthcare Psychologist in Training): Until January 1, 2013, the entry-level scale of the lowest functional scale for psychologists applies. From January 1, 2013, the lowest functional scale for psychologists applies. Salary determination is based on actual hours worked. They have the right to interrupt work for in-school learning (return day, minimum 160 hours/year) with salary retention, pro-rata for part-timers."

      "Article 2:1j Mandatory Participation: The employment contract must state that the employee is subject to mandatory participation in the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn."
    ],
    [
      "Article 2:10 Pensioen Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn (PFZW): The rights and obligations of employer and employee regarding the pension scheme, including the annual premium, are regulated by the pension regulations of Stichting Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn. The pension premium is split 50% for the employer and 50% for the employee, with the employee's portion recovered by the employer."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8A:2.8 (PBL until Jan 1, 2012) Accrued PBL leave cannot be fully taken prior to the pensionable age. To prevent the fiscal consequences of an early retirement scheme, the annual working hours may be reduced by a maximum of 50% compared to the previous calendar year. This condition does not apply to employees who could not use PBL hours due to compelling business interests."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8A:5.3 (PBL until Jan 1, 2012) For PBL from the life-course savings scheme used to finance unpaid leave, the employer pays the employer's contribution to the pension premium and, if applicable, the employer's contribution to the basic supplementary IZZ health insurance scheme."

To prevent the fiscal consequences of an early retirement scheme, the annual working hours may be reduced by a maximum of 50% compared to the previous calendar year. This condition does not apply to employees who could not use PBL hours due to compelling business interests."

    [
      "Article 11:4.4 Holiday Allowance during Illness: During periods of continued salary payment due to illness, the employee retains the right to holiday allowance, after deduction of any holiday allowance from statutory insurance or the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn pension scheme. The holiday allowance is determined according to Article 4:10 and the salary definition in Article 1:1d."

    ],

    ],
    [
      "Article 15:3.5 Pension during Waiting Allowance: During the waiting allowance period, pension entitlements (if voluntary continuation of participation in the applicable pension scheme is possible) remain related to the salary, which is the contribution basis as defined in Article 5 of the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn regulations. The premium for these pension entitlements is borne by the employer, with the employee owing a contribution to the employer related to the amount of the waiting allowance, reduced by any allowances included in the waiting allowance that are not part of the contribution basis."

  "leave_information": [
    [
      "Article 1:1g Feestdagen (Public Holidays) Definition: New Year's Day, Easter Monday, Ascension Day, Whit Monday, Christmas Day and Boxing Day, May 5, and national holidays recognized by the government, as well as special holidays determined by the employer in consultation with the works council."
    ],
    [
      "Article 6:4 Jaarurensystematiek en Feest- en Gedenkdagen (Annual Hours System and Public Holidays): The annual working hours are reduced by 7.2 hours for each public holiday not falling on a Saturday or Sunday. For part-time employees, the pro-rata principle applies. Employees can exchange public holidays for holidays of their own conviction. The employer can make a different arrangement with the works council's consent. If no annual hours system applies, employees are entitled to paid leave for public holidays, and the employer makes an arrangement for employees in rotating shifts with the works council."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:1 Vakantierechten (Vacation Rights) (until January 1, 2012): Employees are entitled to 166 vacation hours per calendar year with salary retention. This is pro-rata for part-time employees or those working more than 1878 hours per year. Employees in service on December 31, 2000, with an average contractual working time of 38 hours (1983 hours per year), have an acquired right to 184 vacation hours per calendar year."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:1 Vakantierechten (Vacation Rights) (from January 1, 2012): The provisions of the Civil Code (Article 7:634 to 645 BW) regarding vacation apply and cannot be deviated from. Employees are entitled to 144 vacation hours per calendar year with salary retention. This is pro-rata for part-time employees or those working more than 1878 hours per year."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:3 Vermindering Vakantierechten (Reduction of Vacation Rights): Vacation leave is proportionally reduced if the employee starts employment during the calendar year, if the employment contract ends during the calendar year, or if the employee works part-time. The resulting vacation leave is rounded up to whole hours."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:4 Geen Opbouw van Vakantiedagen, indien geen recht op loon (No Accrual of Vacation Days if No Right to Salary) (until January 1, 2012): Employees do not accrue vacation for periods during which they do not receive a fixed monetary wage due to not performing work. Exceptions for accrual include fulfilling legal obligations (e.g., military service, not as a profession), leave as per Article 7:641 BW, participation in union meetings with employer consent, or leave as per Article 7:643 BW. Female employees accrue vacation during pregnancy or childbirth leave for the period they receive a pregnancy or childbirth benefit (Wet Arbeid en Zorg). Young employees accrue vacation for time spent on legally required education. Employees unable to work due to illness or accident (unless intentional) accrue vacation based on what they would have accrued if not disabled, with a maximum accrual over the last six months of absence. This also applies to partially disabled employees, if permitted by Article 7:635 BW."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:6 Opname Vakantie en Aftrek Ziektedagen (Taking Vacation and Deduction of Sick Days): Vacation is granted according to the employee's wishes, uninterrupted if desired, unless compelling institutional interests prevent it. Employees can claim at least 3 consecutive weeks of vacation, including the preceding and following weekends. The employer can arrange for a maximum of 3 sick days to be considered vacation leave if the employee takes vacation during a period of disability with the consent of the controlling physician, provided the employee retains at least the statutory minimum number of vacation days (this provision expires on January 1, 2012). The general regulation and distribution of vacation within the institution require the consent of the works council/personnel representation."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:7 Verplichte Collectieve Sluiting (Compulsory Collective Closure): For employees in day care centers, if the employer has established a closure regulation after consultation with employees, employees are obliged to take vacation days during the closure period(s), unless their work prevents it."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:8 Intrekken Vakantie (Revoking Vacation): Granted vacation can be revoked if compelling institutional interests urgently require it. Employees are compensated for any financial damage incurred due to the revocation. If an employee enjoyed only partial vacation on a specific day due to revocation, that day is not counted in the calculation of days taken. If an employee is demonstrably unable to work due to illness (Article 7:629 BW) during a scheduled vacation, those days are not considered vacation days."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:9 Opname in Kalenderjaar (Taking in Calendar Year): Vacation should generally be taken in the relevant calendar year. In exceptional situations, employer and employee can agree to deviate from this."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:10 Omzetting Vakantie in Geld (Conversion of Vacation to Money): Except for the case mentioned in Article 7:641 BW, the right to vacation is not converted into a claim for a monetary payment."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:11 Bovenwettelijke Vakantiedagen (Above-Statutory Vacation Days) (until January 1, 2012): Above-statutory vacation hours from a calendar year can be used for the multi-choice system of employment conditions (Chapter 12)."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:11 Bovenwettelijke Vakantiedagen uit Voorgaande Kalenderjaren (Above-Statutory Vacation Days from Previous Calendar Years) (from January 1, 2012): Above-statutory vacation hours from previous calendar years can be used for the multi-choice system of employment conditions (Chapter 12)."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:12 Definities Verlof (Definitions of Leave): Paid leave refers to hours in a work schedule or roster during which no work is required, and these hours count towards the total working time. Unpaid leave refers to the right to be free from all duties, including night presence, on-call, and standby duties. Children for whom an adoption application has been submitted are considered the employee's children. The pro-rata principle applies to part-time employees."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8:13 Onbetaald Verlof in Verband met Bijzondere Gebeurtenissen (Unpaid Leave for Special Events): The employer grants unpaid leave for: employee relocation; marriage notification; registered partnership notification; notarized cohabitation agreement; attending meetings of Provincial States, Municipal Council, Regional Council, or Water Board as a member; preparing for an indicated exam in the training agreement (max 4 half days or 2 full days per year directly preceding the exam)."

For events a-f, the employee must notify the employer 14 days in advance. For events a-f and h, the pro-rata principle for part-timers does not apply. The employer grants 24 hours of paid leave per year for illness in the family requiring the employee to arrange care, with a regulation established in consultation with the works council."

The employer grants these extra vacation days only once for the same cohabitation. From January 1, 2012, 14.4 hours of extra PBL leave are granted instead of vacation days. In deviation from Article 4:2 Wet Arbeid en Zorg, an employee is entitled to 28.8 hours of maternity leave for the childbirth of their spouse or relation partner. If this coincides with calamity leave (Article 4:1 Wet Arbeid en Zorg), the duration of the calamity leave is deducted. For assistant doctors in training and employees with an average working time of 38 hours (Article 6:1 lid 2 and 5), 16 and 32 hours respectively apply instead of 14.4 and 28.8 hours."

Employer and employee must make an agreement regarding this leave at least 3 months before the estimated date of childbirth. The employee must communicate her wish in a timely manner to the employer."

      "Article 8:21 Ouderschapsverlof (Parental Leave): Employees are entitled to parental leave until their child reaches the age of 8 years, in accordance with Articles 6:1 to 6:9 of the Wet Arbeid en Zorg. Employees can voluntarily continue their pension and/or health insurance. The employer covers a portion of the premiums, provided the employee continues their employment for at least 6 months after the unpaid leave. The employer's share of the premiums is, as far as legally possible, equal to the amount of the employer's contribution due immediately prior to the unpaid leave, but not more than the full premiums due to the pension fund or health insurer during the period of unpaid leave. Employees receive a compensation of 25% of their salary for parental leave taken during the child's first year of life. If the employment contract ends during or within 6 months after the end of parental leave at the employee's request, or if working hours are reduced at the employee's request during this period, the employee must repay the paid salary for the parental leave taken. No vacation leave is accrued during the hours of parental leave."

PBL can be taken as leave, saved in time, or converted to money for a life-course savings account. The employee makes their choice before the calendar year."

As of January 1, 2012, this is 52 hours per calendar year. From January 1, 2013, it is 57 hours per calendar year. PBL is accrued monthly and pro-rata for periods with a right to salary. PBL can be taken as leave, saved in time, or converted to money for a life-course savings account. The employee makes their choice before the calendar year."

      "Article 8A:2 Uitgangspunten Persoonlijk Budget Levensfase (PBL Principles) (until January 1, 2012): PBL aims to promote sustainable employability, discussed in annual reviews. PBL is accrued for the first six months of illness/disability; no accrual thereafter for disabled hours. The employer administers time-saved PBL, providing an annual overview. PBL cannot be cashed out mid-term, only upon termination of employment or death. PBL hours (including transitional rights) can only be used for life-course savings and supplementary pension in the multi-choice system. For on-call workers and part-timers' overtime, PBL hours are paid as a surcharge on the hourly wage, unless otherwise agreed. PBL has no statute of limitations. Accrued PBL leave cannot be fully taken before pensionable age; annual working hours may be reduced by max 50% to avoid early retirement tax consequences, unless compelling business interests prevented PBL use."

    [
      "Article 8A:2 Uitgangspunten Persoonlijk Budget Levensfase (PBL Principles) (from January 1, 2012): PBL aims to promote sustainable employability, discussed in annual reviews. PBL is accrued for the first six months of illness/disability; no accrual thereafter for disabled hours. The employer administers time-saved PBL, providing an annual overview. PBL cannot be cashed out mid-term, only upon termination of employment or death. 22 PBL hours can be used for all purposes in the multi-choice system; remaining PBL hours (including transitional rights) can only be used for life-course savings and supplementary pension. For on-call workers and part-timers' overtime, PBL hours are paid as a surcharge on the hourly wage, unless otherwise agreed. PBL has no statute of limitations. Accrued PBL leave cannot be fully taken before pensionable age; annual working hours may be reduced by max 50% to avoid early retirement tax consequences, unless compelling business interests prevented PBL use."
    ],
    [
      "Article 8A:3 Opname PBL (Taking PBL): PBL can be taken at the employee's discretion on a 'first in-first out' basis. For substantial PBL leave, the employee must submit a written request at least four months in advance. For non-substantial PBL leave, a written request must be submitted within a reasonable period. The employer grants the request unless a compelling business interest reasonably outweighs the employee's interest. If PBL leave coincides with illness, the PBL leave is suspended for the duration of the illness, and a new end date is agreed upon. A doctor's certificate is required if requested by the employer."

PBL from any calendar year can be saved value-indexed for a maximum of five years at the value of the current hourly wage. If PBL is not taken within five years, the employee chooses to either leave it as a life-phase credit with the employer at that moment's hourly wage (without further indexation) or convert it to money and deposit it into their life-course savings account."

      "Article 8A:5 Sparen via de Levensloopregeling (Geldsparen) (Saving via the Life-Course Savings Scheme (Money Saving)): Employees can convert accrued PBL into money for their life-course savings account once a year at the end of the calendar year. The conversion is based on the current hourly wage at the end of the calendar year, increased by holiday allowance. If PBL is no longer indexed after five years, the deposit is based on the fixed monetary value. The money from converted PBL hours is deposited into a separate life-course savings account used only for PBL deposits and withdrawals. New PBL is accrued pro-rata for PBL from the life-course savings scheme used to finance unpaid leave. The employer pays the employer's contribution to the pension premium and, if applicable, the employer's contribution to the basic supplementary IZZ health insurance scheme for PBL used to finance unpaid leave. If illness/disability lasts longer than seven days during PBL leave financed by the life-course savings scheme, the remaining PBL is suspended for the duration of the disability, and a new end date is agreed upon. A doctor's certificate is required if requested by the employer. The employer is responsible for re-staffing the hours of PBL taken. The employer consults with the OR on the choice of a life-course savings provider and informs the employee about the consequences of participating in the life-course savings scheme for choices in the multi-choice system, taking timely action to avoid fiscal reassessment."

  "termination_information": [
    [
      "Article 2:4 Verlengde Arbeidsovereenkomsten voor Bepaalde Tijd (Extended Fixed-Term Employment Contracts): Fixed-term contracts can be extended according to Article 7:668a of the Civil Code. For temporary replacement, more than 3 consecutive fixed-term contracts (even with breaks of 3 months or less) can be concluded without creating an indefinite-term contract, provided a term of 2.5 years is not exceeded. If a fixed-term contract for training ends, a new contract for the remaining duration of the training can be entered into without creating an indefinite-term contract if the extension is necessary to complete the training."
    ],
    [
      "Article 2:5 Einde van de Arbeidsovereenkomst (Termination of the Employment Contract): An employment contract ends: by mutual consent; when the employee reaches the AOW-eligible age (from January 1, 2012, this is the day before reaching AOW-eligible age, unless otherwise agreed); by operation of law (expiry of term or completion of work); for an indefinite-term contract, by notice (Article 2:6); by termination for urgent reasons (Articles 7:678 and 7:679 BW); by dissolution by the subdistrict court (Article 7:685 BW); immediately by unilateral termination by the employer or employee during a written agreed probation period; by operation of law upon the death of the employee; on the first day of the following calendar month if the employee wishes to terminate due to childbirth and notifies the employer in writing within 10 calendar days after birth; at the time of using the bridging benefit under the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn pension scheme, unless otherwise agreed (employee must notify employer in time)."
    ],
    [
      "Article 2:6 Opzegging (Notice of Termination): For termination as per Article 2:5 lid 1d or if a fixed-term contract includes a clause for interim termination, statutory notice periods apply unless explicitly stated otherwise in this CAO. Employer's notice must be in writing. If the employee's notice period is extended, the notice periods for both employee and employer are equal if not longer than 3 months. If longer than 3 months, the employer's notice period is double that of the employee. The effective date of dismissal is the first day of the calendar month."
    ],
    [
      "Article 2:7 Op Non-Actiefstelling (Suspension from Duty): The employer can suspend an employee for a maximum of 3 weeks if the progress of work is seriously hindered. The period should be as short as possible but long enough to find solutions. If no solution is found after 3 weeks, the period can be extended by a maximum of 6 weeks after consultation with the employee or their counsel. If external causes necessitate it, the employer can extend this period for longer than 6 weeks with motivation. Once a dismissal procedure has started, the employer can extend the suspension for a period determined by them, until the employment contract ends or the dismissal procedure is concluded. The decision and any extension must be communicated to the employee in writing with reasons. Suspension is always with retention of all other rights from the employment contract/CAO. The employer must promote the employee's return to work. The employee is entitled to resume work after the suspension period. Suspension cannot be used as a disciplinary measure."
    ],
    [
      "Article 2:8 Schorsing (Disciplinary Suspension): The employer can suspend an employee for a maximum of one week with salary retention for reasons so serious that continued work by the employee is deemed irresponsible. The suspension must be communicated or confirmed immediately, with motivation, by registered letter. The employee must be given the opportunity to respond to the employer within 4 days (excluding Saturdays, Sundays, and public holidays) and can be assisted by counsel. The employer can extend the suspension once for a maximum of one week, but cannot extend it simultaneously with the initial suspension. If a dismissal procedure has been initiated with UWV WERKbedrijf or a request for dissolution of the employment contract has been submitted to the court or arbitration tribunal, the suspension can be extended until the effective date of dismissal or termination of the employment contract. The employee retains their salary during an extended suspension. The employer is authorized to deny the employee access to the institution's buildings and grounds, provided it does not concern the employee's living space. If it appears that the employee was wrongly suspended, the employer will publicly rehabilitate them and compensate for demonstrable damage upon request."
    ],
    [
      "Article 2:9 Uitkering bij Overlijden (Death Benefit): In case of an employee's death, the employer provides a benefit to the spouse or relation partner (not permanently separated), or in their absence, to minor children, or in their absence, to the person largely supported by the employee and living in a family context. The benefit is based on the last earned salary for the period from the day after death until the last day of the third full calendar month after the month of death. The death benefit is paid tax and premium-free, except for the salary of the month of death. If the deceased leaves no such relations, the employer can pay the benefit (or part of it) to person(s) deemed equitable. The death benefit is reduced by any death benefits received under the Ziektewet, Wet op de Arbeidsongeschiktheidsverzekering, or Wet Werk en Inkomen naar Arbeidsvermogen."
    ],
    [
      "Article 15:1 Recht op Wachtgeld (Right to Waiting Allowance): An employee with an indefinite-term contract who is dismissed solely due to: a. reduction or termination of work; b. reorganization; or c. incompetence or unsuitability for the function (not due to their fault, but due to organizational, function, or professional developments outside the employee's person, and not disability), is granted a waiting allowance from the date of dismissal according to this CAO. The waiting allowance is only granted if the employee receives a benefit under the Werkloosheidswet due to dismissal for one of the aforementioned reasons. Dissolution of the employment contract under Article 7:685 BW for these reasons is considered equivalent to dismissal, but there can be no overlap between waiting allowance and dissolution compensation if the court has already considered the waiting allowance scheme in its decision."
    ],
    [
      "Article 15:2 Duur van het Wachtgeld (Duration of Waiting Allowance): The waiting allowance is granted for three months. The duration is extended by 1 month for each full year of continuous service beyond 3 years. The total duration of the waiting allowance cannot exceed 38 months. For determining service years, all full months of continuous service with an employer under this CAO count. For employees in service on December 31, 2000, under the CAO Gehandicaptenzorg (formerly Ziekenhuiswezen), all full continuous months of service with institutions affiliated with the former Nederlandse Zorgfederatie or its predecessors also count."
    ],
    [
      "Article 15:3 Hoogte van het Wachtgeld (Amount of Waiting Allowance): The waiting allowance is 100% of the last earned salary for the first 6 months, then 80% for the next 3 months, 75% for the subsequent 2 years, and 70% thereafter. During the waiting allowance period, it is never less than 80% of the minimum wage (Wet Minimumloon en Minimum Vakantiebijslag). If the recipient is eligible for a bridging benefit under the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn regulations, this benefit is supplemented to the waiting allowance amount. The last earned salary is calculated based on the daily wage rules for employee insurance, including general salary increases that would have been received if employment had continued. If a gradual or partial termination of employment would lead to unfavorable results for the recipient, this provision may be deviated from in their favor. During the waiting allowance period, pension entitlements (if voluntary continuation is possible) remain related to the salary (contribution basis Article 5 PFZW). The employer pays the premium, with the employee contributing based on the waiting allowance (minus allowances not included in the contribution basis)."
    ],
    [
      "Article 15:4 Verplichtingen van de Rechthebbende (Obligations of the Recipient): The recipient must register with UWV WERKbedrijf as soon as possible, but no later than 14 days after notification of dismissal, and maintain this registration. The recipient must accept any suitable opportunity to earn income from work. The recipient must immediately report any income from work or business, as well as any benefits received under statutory regulations, to the employer, and provide all requested information and proof."
    ],
    [
      "Article 15:5 Vermindering van het Wachtgeld (Reduction of Waiting Allowance): If the recipient earns income from work or business on or after the start date of the waiting allowance, this income is deducted if, together with the waiting allowance, it exceeds 103% of the last earned salary during the first 2 years, and 93% thereafter. The waiting allowance is reduced by any benefits received under the Werkloosheidswet, Ziektewet, Wet op de Arbeidsongeschiktheidsverzekering, Wet Werk en Inkomen naar Arbeidsvermogen, Wet Arbeidsongeschiktheidsvoorziening Jonggehandicapten, and Wet Arbeidsongeschiktheidsvoorziening Zelfstandigen. A pension claimed at the time of dismissal (Article 15:1) due to reaching the pensionable age under an applicable pension scheme is considered income. If the recipient's unemployment benefit is reduced due to their own fault, the waiting allowance is proportionally reduced."
    ],
    [
      "Article 15:6 Opschorting van het Wachtgeld (Suspension of Waiting Allowance): For a recipient of waiting allowance who is or must enter military service under a statutory obligation, or is or will be employed under Article 9 of the Wet Gewetensbezwaarden Militaire Dienst, the further execution of this scheme is suspended for the duration of that service or employment, upon request."
    ],
    [
      "Article 15:7 Vervallen van het Wachtgeld (Termination of Waiting Allowance): The waiting allowance terminates: from the day following the recipient's death; from the day the recipient claims old-age pension or disability pension under the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn regulations, arising from the employment from which they were dismissed with a right to waiting allowance; if the recipient refuses to comply with any of the obligations imposed in Article 15:4; from the day the recipient no longer receives a WW-uitkering. The waiting allowance can be declared terminated by the employer if: the recipient does not sufficiently cooperate with a medical examination for applying for a disability pension or a statutory disability benefit; the recipient can be deemed to have permanently settled abroad (this does not apply to individuals who have reached the age of 55)."
    ],

The benefit is equal to the waiting allowance that would have been paid over the next 3 months. This benefit ceases if death benefits from social insurance laws are equal to or higher. If the deceased leaves no such relations, the employer can pay the benefit to the person(s) designated in writing by the employee to the employer."
    ],

      "Article 15:9 Uitbetaling van het Wachtgeld (Payment of Waiting Allowance): The waiting allowance is paid monthly in accordance with Article 4:7."
    ],
    [
      "Article 15:10 Afkoop van het Wachtgeld (Buyout of Waiting Allowance): After receiving waiting allowance for 9 months, the recipient is offered the option to capitalize any remaining claim to waiting allowance (the supplement to the WW-uitkering) at the then-current statutory interest rate and receive the determined amount as a lump sum. Exercising this buyout option terminates the remaining claim to waiting allowance. The amount can be used for direct spending, a life-course savings scheme, or a reintegration budget, provided it is fiscally favorable for both the recipient and the employer."

  "overtime_information": [
    [
      "Article 6:6.2 Arbeids- en Rusttijden (Work and Rest Times): The employer can assign irregular shifts (weekend, public holidays, evening and night shifts), on-call duties (bereikbaarheidsdiensten), standby duties (consignatiediensten), consultation duties (consultatiediensten), and (night)presence duties (nachtaanwezigheidsdiensten), and assign overtime (overwerk)."
    ],
    [
      "Article 6:8 Verschoven Diensten (Shifted Shifts): If, due to incidental special circumstances, business interests require it, the employer can, after consultation with the employee, change an already established work schedule (Article 6:6 lid 1). The employee receives compensation according to Article 7:12. A shifted shift occurs when a continuous period of hours, during which the employee was scheduled to work, is moved by the employer to another time when the employee was not scheduled to work."
    ],
    [
      "Article 6:10.1 Arbeidstijden Zwangere Werkneemsters en Oudere Werknemers (Working Hours for Pregnant Employees and Older Employees): A pregnant employee cannot be obliged to perform overtime, irregular shifts, on-call duties, standby duties, consultation duties, or (night)presence duties."
    ],
    [
      "Article 6:10.2 Arbeidstijden Zwangere Werkneemsters en Oudere Werknemers (Working Hours for Pregnant Employees and Older Employees): An employee aged 57 and older cannot be obliged to perform night shifts. Night shifts are defined as shifts where more than one hour of work is performed between 00:00 and 06:00, not being a night presence duty/sleep duty."
    ],
    [
      "Article 6:10.3 Arbeidstijden Zwangere Werkneemsters en Oudere Werknemers (Working Hours for Pregnant Employees and Older Employees): An employee aged 57 or older who chooses to be structurally scheduled for night shifts works 40 hours less per year (pro-rata to the employment size)."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:1 Definities Overwerk, Onregelmatige Dienst, Bereikbaarheidsdienst, Consignatiedienst, Nachtaanwezigheidsdienst, Consultatiedienst (Definitions of Overtime, Irregular Shift, On-call Duty, Standby Duty, Night Presence Duty, Consultation Duty): Overtime is incidental work performed beyond the established working hours in the work and rest time pattern, with the exceeding of working hours measured on a semi-annual basis. It does not include shifted shifts. An on-call duty is a continuous period of up to 24 hours during which the employee must be available to perform work if called, possibly in addition to their regular work. A standby duty is a period between two consecutive shifts or during a break, during which the employee is only required to be available to perform work as soon as possible if called due to unforeseen circumstances. An irregular shift is work performed according to a schedule during the hours mentioned in Article 7:11. It also includes work performed by a part-time employee not according to schedule during the hours mentioned in Article 7:11, exceeding their contractually agreed hours, provided it does not exceed 1878 hours per year. A night presence duty is a continuous 24-hour period, including a continuous period of at least 6 and a maximum of 8 hours between 23:00 and 09:00, during which the employee must be present and available in the institution for necessary and unforeseen work, but otherwise rests. A consultation duty is the circumstance that an employee must be available by telephone for consultation outside of established working hours."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:2 Opdracht tot Overwerk (Overtime Assignment): Compensation for overtime is given if the employee has received an assignment for overtime, or could reasonably assume that they would have received an assignment for overtime. In such a case, the employer retrospectively determines the necessity of the overtime."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:3 Bepaling Aantal Overwerkuren (Determining Number of Overtime Hours): If overtime is performed for a period of half an hour or less preceding or immediately following the scheduled working hours, this period is not eligible for compensation. If overtime is performed for a period longer than half an hour, this period is rounded up to a full hour. If overtime is performed for a period longer than one hour, this period is rounded up to half or full hours."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:4 Vergoedingsregeling voor Werknemer met een Volletijd Arbeidsduur (Compensation Scheme for Full-time Employees for Overtime): Compensation for overtime is provided in the form of free time, equal to the number of hours worked, and additionally in the form of a monetary payment. The monetary payment is a percentage of the hourly wage: 25% for overtime performed between 06:00 and 22:00 from Monday to Friday (with a maximum of 5 hours in a seven-day period, other hours at 50%); 50% for overtime performed between 22:00 and 06:00 from Monday to Friday; 75% for overtime performed on Saturday until 18:00 and on free days; 100% for overtime performed on Saturday from 18:00, on Sundays and public holidays between 00:00 and 24:00, and on December 24 and 31 between 18:00 and 24:00. Free days are defined as days, not being Sunday or a public holiday, on which the employee would not have to work according to their work and rest time pattern. The right to overtime compensation is granted if the employee's salary does not exceed insertion number 48 of the salary table, or if it exceeds insertion number 48 and the number of hours worked above the scheduled working hours averages more than 6 per week (measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies). CAO parties can set a different hour norm for overtime for specific categories of employees."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:5 Vergoedingsregeling voor de Deeltijdwerker (Compensation Scheme for Part-time Employees for Overtime): Overtime compensation consists of the employee's applicable hourly wage if the number of overtime hours, measured over a six-month period, does not exceed the difference between the employee's contractual working hours and full-time working hours. Beyond that difference, compensation as specified in Article 7:4 is granted."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:6 Maximaal Aantal Uren Overwerk, Vacaturestelling (Maximum Overtime Hours, Vacancy Posting): The number of overtime hours, measured per continuous period of one year, may not exceed 10% of 1878 hours if the employee's salary is at or below insertion number 48, or 10% of 2191 hours if the salary exceeds insertion number 48. For employees with a contract of more than 1878 hours, their contractual hours are used instead of 1878, and their contractual hours plus 313 hours instead of 2191. If the 10% limit is exceeded, the employer must provide assistance or post a vacancy upon the employee's request. The works council receives a quarterly overview of overtime hours per employee/department to assess the policy on vacancy posting or assistance."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:7 Structureel Meerwerk (Structural Additional Work): If a part-time employee works more than 10% above their contractually agreed working hours over a reference period of one year, the employer must offer them a contract that incorporates these additional hours, upon the employee's request. If the employee does not request this, assistance will be provided or a vacancy will be posted."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:8 Opnemen Vergoeding Overwerk (Taking Overtime Compensation): The free time mentioned in Article 7:4 must be granted and taken no later than the quarter following the reference period in which the overtime was performed, unless explicitly agreed otherwise between employer and employee. If the employer deems that institutional interests prevent granting free time, the free time can be converted into a monetary amount (up to half) with the employee's consent, consisting of a pro-rata portion of the salary. The works council receives insight into the application of this provision upon request."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:9 Werkingssfeer Vergoeding Onregelmatige Dienst (Scope of Irregular Shift Compensation): Employees classified in job group 65 or lower are entitled to compensation for performing irregular shifts."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:10 Vergoedingsregeling Onregelmatige Dienst (Irregular Shift Compensation Scheme): Compensation for irregular shifts is provided as a monetary payment, or as free time if the employee requests it. The free time is calculated by dividing the monetary compensation (Article 7:11) by the employee's hourly wage. The request for free time is granted unless institutional interests prevent it. The request must be made in the first half of a calendar year, and if granted, the free time compensation starts on January 1 of the following year and remains in effect for at least one calendar year. By June 30 of any calendar year, employer and employee can notify that compensation should be monetary instead of free time, effective January 1 of the following year. The employer can refuse the employee's notification if institutional interests prevent it. If an irregular shift is terminated or reduced, or if this occurs based on occupational health advice, the employer can agree on a temporary phase-out scheme for the irregular shift compensation with the employee."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:11 Berekening Geldelijke Vergoeding Onregelmatige Dienst (Calculation of Monetary Irregular Shift Compensation): The monetary compensation mentioned in Article 7:10 is calculated based on the applicable hourly wage, with a maximum hourly wage derived from insertion number 19 of the salary table (Article 4:31), using the following percentages: 22% for irregular shifts between 06:00 and 07:00 and between 20:00 and 22:00 from Monday to Friday; 38% for hours between 06:00 and 08:00 and between 12:00 and 22:00 on Saturday; 44% for hours between 00:00 and 06:00 and between 22:00 and 24:00 from Monday to Friday; 49% for hours between 00:00 and 06:00 and between 22:00 and 24:00 on Saturday; 60% for hours between 00:00 and 24:00 on Sundays and public holidays, and for hours between 18:00 and 24:00 on December 24 and 31."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:12 Vergoeding Verschoven Dienst (Shifted Shift Compensation): If the employer applies Article 6:8 regarding shifted shifts and thereby changes an already established work schedule, the employee receives compensation if they have already incurred expenses for leisure activities. If the employer applies Article 6:8 and a shift occurs within 48 hours of notification, the employee receives, in addition to the compensation in lid 1, the hourly wage for the shifted hours plus compensation according to Article 7:4 lid 2."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:13 Vergoeding voor de Bereikbaarheids-, Consignatie-, en Consultatiedienst (Compensation for On-call, Standby, and Consultation Duty): Employees whose salary does not exceed the amount indicated at insertion number 88 of the salary table receive compensation in free time for hours spent on on-call, standby, and consultation duty. The compensation per hour is: 3/18 on recognized public holidays; 2/18 on Saturdays/Sundays; 1/18 on other days. If an employee performs more than 8 weekend on-call, standby, or consultation duties in 3 consecutive 28-day periods (observing Article 6:6), they receive a 50% surcharge on this compensation for the additional weekend duties."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:14 (Opnemen) Vergoeding Bereikbaarheids-, Consignatie- en Consultatiedienst ((Taking) Compensation for On-call, Standby, and Consultation Duty): The free time obtained under Articles 7:13 and 7:15 must be granted and taken within a period of 2 months after performing the duty, unless otherwise agreed. If institutional interests prevent granting free time, up to half of the free time can be converted into a monetary amount, consisting of a pro-rata portion of the salary. If a duty is performed on an employer-designated vacation day (Articles 8:6 lid 3 and 8:7), the employee's claim to that day remains."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:15 Vergoeding van Arbeid tijdens Bereikbaarheids-, Consignatie- en Consultatiedienst (Compensation for Work during On-call, Standby, and Consultation Duty): Compensation for work performed during these duties is provided in the form of free time, equal to the number of hours worked, and additionally in the form of a monetary payment. The monetary payment is a percentage of the hourly wage: 25% for work performed between 06:00 and 22:00 from Monday to Friday (with a maximum of 5 hours in a seven-day period, other hours at 50%); 50% for work performed between 22:00 and 06:00 from Monday to Friday; 75% for work performed on Saturday until 18:00 and on free days; 100% for work performed on Saturday from 18:00, on Sundays and public holidays between 00:00 and 24:00, and on December 24 and 31 between 18:00 and 24:00. Free days are defined as days, not being Sunday or a public holiday, on which the employee would not have to work according to their work schedule or roster. If called during standby duty, compensation is calculated for at least half an hour, plus actual travel time. If called (telephonically) during on-call duty, compensation is calculated for at least half an hour, plus any travel time."
    ],
    [
      "Article 7:16 Maximaal Aantal Bereikbaarheids-, Consignatie- of Consultatiediensten (Maximum Number of On-call, Standby, or Consultation Duties): An employee can be scheduled for a maximum of 7 days of on-call and standby duties in a 28-day period, with a maximum of 5 consecutive days, provided this is permitted by the Arbeidstijdenwet/Arbeidstijdenbesluit. Consultation duties can be performed for a maximum of 7 consecutive days, provided this is permitted by the Arbeidstijdenwet/Arbeidstijdenbesluit."

    ],
    [
      "Article 7:18 Nachtaanwezigheidsdienst (Night Presence Duty): Periods during which an employee performs necessary work during a night presence duty (whether on call or not), if they total no more than 120 minutes, are considered a continuation of the night presence duty. If these periods total no more than 120 minutes, the employee is compensated based on Article 7:19 lid 1. If these periods total more than 120 minutes, the duty is not considered a night presence duty but is entirely classified as working time, and the employee is compensated based on Article 7:19 lid 2."
    ],

      "Article 7:19 Compensatie Nachtaanwezigheidsdienst (Night Presence Duty Compensation): Hours spent on night presence duty are considered half working hours and are compensated in time. At the employee's request, this time compensation can be paid out at the employee's current hourly wage at the time of performing the night presence duty. If, based on Article 7:18 lid 3, the night presence duty is not considered as such but as full working time, the employee is compensated in time. At the employee's request, this time compensation can be paid out at the employee's current hourly wage at the time of performing the night presence duty."

If employment ends before December 31, the employee is entitled to a pro-rata year-end bonus. The employee is entitled to a year-end bonus only once per actually earned salary in any calendar year. In December 2023, the year-end bonus will be increased once by 0.5% to 8.83%."
    ],
    [
      "Salary payment (Article 4:7): Employees must have access to their salary for the month no later than 2 days before the end of the calendar month (excluding Sundays and public holidays). Reimbursements as specified in chapters 7 and 9 are paid no later than the end of the calendar month following the month in which the entitlements arose. Employees receive written notification of any changes in salary and salary calculation."
    ],
    [
      "Periodic increases (Article 4:8): Unless otherwise specified in the employment contract, a salary increase within the salary scale is granted once per year. The first periodic increase is granted 1 year after hiring or promotion to a function classified in a higher function group. The employer can decide not to grant a salary increase or to grant multiple increases in a year, if a performance appraisal system (which must be transparent, allow appeal, and offer consultation for negative appraisals) provides grounds for it."
    ],
    [
      "Special allowances (Article 4:9):",
      "- Gratification: The employer can grant a gratification.",
      "- Labor market shortage allowance: In special cases, a temporary allowance of up to 10% of the employee's salary can be granted for specific function(s) experiencing a labor market shortage.",
      "- Special circumstances allowance: An allowance can be granted if, in the employer's judgment, there are special circumstances regarding the performance of the function. The sum of the employee's salary and this allowance cannot exceed the maximum of the next higher salary scale, but the allowance itself cannot exceed 8 consecutive IP-numbers from the employee's current salary."
    ],
    [
      "Holiday allowance (Article 4:10): The holiday allowance for an employee who has been employed for a full year on May 31 is 8% of the actual salary earned from June 1 to May 31 of the preceding year. The holiday allowance is calculated at least based on IP-number 8, pro-rata to the employment size. It is paid once a year in May. If employment is less than a full year or ends before May 31, the allowance is pro-rata. This provision is not mandatory for employees with fixed-term employment contracts."
    ],
    [
      "Promotion (Article 4:11): Upon promotion to a function classified in a higher function group, the salary is taken from the salary scale of the new function, with the understanding that the previously earned salary is increased by 2 periodic steps, and the new salary must be at least the minimum of the salary scale of the promoted function, but never more than the maximum of the scale belonging to this higher function. The employer can fully deviate from this with the consent of the works council/personnel representation by establishing its own regulation. This also applies to an Arts voor Verstandelijk Gehandicapten who, after completing their training in SBOH employment, enters into an employment contract with the employer where they were employed prior to their training."
    ],
    [
      "Acting allowance (Waarneming) (Article 4:12): An employee designated to act in a higher-classified function for more than 1 month continuously (other than for vacation replacement), either entirely or almost entirely, or for at least half of the daily working hours, receives an allowance for each month of acting. This allowance is equal to the difference between their current salary and the starting salary of the higher-classified function, with a minimum of 2 periodic steps. The salary plus the aforementioned allowance cannot exceed the maximum of the scale of the higher-classified function being acted in. For acting for at least half of the daily working hours, the allowance is calculated pro-rata. The employer can fully deviate from this with the consent of the works council/personnel representation by establishing its own regulation."
    ],
    [
      "Jubilee gratification (Article 4:13): An employee who has been continuously employed by the employer is entitled to a one-time gratification of one-quarter of the monthly salary at 12.5 years, half of the monthly salary at 25 years, and a full monthly salary at 40 years of service. Upon termination of the employment contract due to reaching the AOW-eligible age, the employee is entitled to a gratification of half of the monthly salary."
    ],
    [
      "Salary consequences after classification change (Article 4:14):",
      "- If a lower function group becomes effective after a change procedure (Annex 4 FWG-procedures), the salary as stipulated in the employment contract is frozen if it exceeds the maximum salary of the lower function group plus 10%. This frozen salary is adjusted with general wage adjustments applicable to the CAO Gehandicaptenzorg. The employee can reach the maximum salary as stipulated in the employment contract, provided it does not exceed the maximum salary plus 10% belonging to the lower function group.",
      "- If a higher function group becomes effective after a change procedure (Annex 4 FWG-procedures), the salary scale belonging to the higher function group becomes applicable. When determining the salary from the new salary scale, the amount that applied on the first day of the aforementioned calendar month is the minimum. If this amount is not present in the new salary scale, the next higher amount in that salary scale applies."
    ],

      "Pension Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn (Article 2:10): The rights and obligations of employer and employee regarding the applicable pension scheme for the employee, including the determination of the annual premium, are regulated in the provisions of the pension regulations of Stichting Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn. The distribution of the pension premium is determined by CAO parties: 50% for the employer and 50% for the employee. The employee's portion is recovered by the employer from the employee."

      "Pension accrual during Career Reduction Facility (Article 8A:12): Pension accrual will be based on the new pensionable salary (85% of original). The employee can apply for voluntary continuation with PFZW, in which case pension accrual remains based on the pensionable salary corresponding to the original working hours (100%). The distribution of pension premiums between employer and employee remains 50/50% in both cases. The employer will support the employee where possible in applying for voluntary continuation."

    [
      "Annual hours system and vacation (Article 6:3): If vacation hours are taken on a day when a certain number of hours would have been worked according to the individual work and rest time pattern, this number of hours is registered as vacation."
