    [
      "Annual hours system and holidays (Article 6:4): The number of hours to be worked annually is reduced by 7.2 hours for each holiday not falling on a Saturday or Sunday. For employees with a working time deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies regarding deployability on holidays and the reduction mentioned in lid 1. For employees starting during the calendar year, the reduction applies to holidays occurring in the remaining calendar year. Employees can exchange holidays for holidays applicable to their life conviction. The employer can, in consultation with the works council, make a deviating arrangement. If no agreement is reached, the provisions of this article remain applicable. If no annual hours system applies (Article 6:1 lid 6), the employee is entitled to leave with retention of salary for holidays as mentioned in Article 1:1 sub g. The employer makes an arrangement for holiday leave for employees working in rotating shifts in consultation with the works council."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation rights (Article 8:1): The provisions of the Civil Code regarding vacation (Article 7:634 to 645 BW) apply, unless otherwise specified or supplemented in this chapter. No deviation from these statutory provisions is allowed. The employee is entitled to 144 vacation hours per calendar year with retention of salary. For part-time employees, the regulation in lid 2 is pro-rata to the employment size. For employees with an average working time exceeding 1878 hours per year, the number of vacation hours is also pro-rata to the employment size. For employees as referred to in Article 7:1 lid 4 juncto 7:9, the hourly wage for taken statutory vacation hours is increased by the average paid ORT (irregularity allowance) over the six months preceding the month in which the vacation is taken."
    ],
    [
      "Reduction of vacation rights (Article 8:3): The duration of vacation established according to Article 8:1 is reduced proportionally in the following cases: if the employee starts employment during the calendar year; if the employment contract ends or is terminated during the calendar year; if the employee works part-time. The vacation leave to which the employee is entitled due to the reduction mentioned in the first paragraph is, if necessary, rounded up to whole hours."
    ],
    [
      "Taking vacation (Article 8:6): Vacation is granted, unless the interests of the department or service where the employee is employed prevent it, in accordance with the employee's wishes – uninterrupted if desired. The employee can claim at least 3 consecutive weeks of vacation, including the weekends preceding and following it. The general regulation and the distribution of vacation within the institution require the consent of the works council/personnel representation."
    ],
    [
      "Mandatory collective closure (Article 8:7): For employees in day care centers, if the employer, after consultation with the employees, has established a closure regulation, employees are obliged to take vacation days during the closure period(s), unless the work prevents it."
    ],
    [
      "Withdrawal of vacation (Article 8:8): Granted vacation can be withdrawn if the interest of the institution urgently necessitates it. If the employee suffers financial damage as a result of the withdrawal of vacation, this will be compensated. If the employee, as a result of the withdrawal of vacation, only enjoyed partial vacation on a certain day, this day is not taken into account in the calculation of the number of days enjoyed. If an employee is demonstrably unfit for work in the sense of Article 7:629 BW during a fixed vacation, these granted vacation days do not count as vacation days."

    [
      "Parental leave (Article 8:21): The employee is entitled to parental leave during the period that the employee's child has not yet reached the age of 8 years, in accordance with the regulation included in Articles 6:1 to 6:9 of the Wet Arbeid en Zorg. The employee can voluntarily continue their pension insurance and/or health insurance. The employer bears a portion of the premiums due for this, provided the employee continues their employment for at least 6 months after the unpaid leave. The employer's portion of the premiums mentioned in the previous paragraph is, as far as legally possible, equal to the amount of the employer's contribution that was due immediately prior to the unpaid leave, but does not exceed the full premiums due to the pension fund or health insurer during the period of unpaid leave. The employee receives a compensation of 25% of the salary for the hours of parental leave taken in the first year of the child for whom the leave is taken. This paragraph expires as of August 2, 2022. If the employment contract is terminated at the employee's request during or within 6 months after the end of (a period of) parental leave, or if a reduction in working hours occurs at the employee's request during this period, the employee must repay the paid salary for the parental leave taken to the employer. This paragraph expires as of August 2, 2022. Vacation leave is accrued for parental leave only for the hours for which the employee receives a benefit based on Article 6:3 of the Wet Arbeid en Zorg."
    ],
    [
      "Personal Budget Life Phase (PBL) (Article 8A:1) (applies until December 31, 2022): The employee receives, in addition to statutory vacation hours, a Personal Budget Life Phase (PBL) of 57 hours per calendar year on a full-time basis. On December 1, 2022, all employees employed on December 1, 2022, will receive a one-time extra 8 hours of PBL, pro-rata to their contractual working hours and the duration of their employment in 2022. The PBL is accrued monthly. The employee accrues PBL hours for each calendar year in which they were entitled to salary for the full agreed working hours, unless otherwise agreed in this CAO. An employee who was entitled to salary for part of the calendar year accrues PBL pro-rata for that part. The PBL (including rights based on transition rights) can be taken by the employee entirely or partially as leave, entirely or partially saved in time, or entirely or partially paid out. The employee who qualifies can convert the PBL entirely or partially into money for deposit into their life-course savings account. The employee makes their choice known in time, prior to the calendar year."
    ],
    [
      "PBL principles (Article 8A:2): The employer facilitates the employee's sustainable employability with the PBL. The employee makes an annual plan for the use of PBL to promote their sustainable employability, which is discussed in the annual review. The employer manages the administration of the PBL for time-saving purposes. The employee receives an annual overview of accrued PBL from the employer. PBL is accrued for the first six months of sickness/disability. Thereafter, no further accrual occurs for the hours of disability. Periods are aggregated if they follow each other with an interruption of less than one month. The employee can request to purchase a maximum of 7 extra days of PBL (50.4 hours, pro-rata for part-timers). Extra PBL hours can only be purchased for use in the calendar year of purchase and are not eligible for sale. A rejection of the request must be motivated in writing. Accrued PBL can be paid out at the end of a calendar year. The employer agrees to the request unless compelling business interests prevent it; in such a case, the payout of PBL hours takes place at a later determined time, but no later than 6 months after the employee's request. Upon termination of employment/death, the remaining PBL is paid out. Upon request of the employee who qualifies, the remaining PBL can be (partially) deposited into their life-course savings account. PBL accrued over the worked hours of a flexible worker and overtime of a part-timer is paid out as a surcharge on the hourly wage, unless employer and employee agree otherwise."
    ],
    [
      "Taking PBL (Article 8A:3): PBL can be taken at the employee's discretion and wishes on a first-in, first-out basis, subject to the following: for substantial PBL leave, the employee submits a written request to the employer at least four months before the start of the leave; for non-substantial PBL leave, the employee submits their written request within a reasonable period; the employer grants the request for leave unless compelling business interests prevent it, requiring the employee's interest to yield based on standards of reasonableness and fairness. In case of concurrent PBL leave and sickness, the PBL leave is suspended for the duration of the sickness. The employer and employee make a new agreement on the end date of the leave. Upon the employer's request, the employee provides a doctor's statement."
    ],
    [
      "Saving PBL with employer (time saving) (Article 8A:4): The PBL can be entirely or partially saved in time with the employer. PBL from any calendar year can be saved value-protected for a maximum of five years at the value of the current hourly wage. If the employee has not taken the PBL (partially) within the five-year period, the PBL is paid out at the end of the calendar year after the indexation period. Deviating from lid 3, the employer and employee can agree otherwise, if the employee wishes, based on a motivated proposal from the employee in the context of their sustainable employability. In that case, the PBL does not expire."
    ],
    [
      "Saving via life-course savings scheme (money saving) (Article 8A:5): An employee who qualifies can convert the accrued PBL into money once a year at the end of the calendar year for deposit into their life-course savings account. The conversion to money takes place based on the current hourly wage at the end of the calendar year, increased by holiday allowance. If the PBL is no longer indexed after the five-year period, the deposit into the life-course savings account is according to the fixed monetary value. The money from the converted PBL hours is deposited into a separate life-course savings account that can only be used for PBL deposits and withdrawals. For PBL from the life-course savings scheme used to finance unpaid leave, new PBL is accrued pro-rata to the taken PBL percentage relative to the employee's current salary. For PBL from the life-course savings scheme used to finance unpaid leave, the employer pays the employer's contribution to the pension premium and, if applicable, the employer's contribution to the IZZ health insurance in accordance with Article 9:8. In case of sickness/disability lasting longer than seven days during PBL leave from the life-course savings scheme used to finance unpaid leave, the remaining portion of the PBL is suspended for the duration of the disability. The employer and employee make a new agreement on the end date of the leave. Upon the employer's request, the employee provides a doctor's statement. The employer is responsible for re-staffing the taken PBL hours."
    ],
    [
      "PBL transition rights principles (Article 8A:6): Transition rights apply exclusively to employees employed on October 1, 2009, by an employer falling under the CAO Gehandicaptenzorg. The date for age determination, for the purpose of the transition scheme, is set at December 31, 2009. PBL transition rights are granted pro-rata to: the contract size at the time of actual granting of the PBL transition right, AND the duration of the employee's employment in any calendar year, AND the (remaining) months of the calendar year that the transition right applies. The amount of the transition right includes the PBL right based on Article 8A:1."

      "PBL transition rights for employees 50-64 on December 31, 2009 (Article 8A:7): Deviating from Article 8A:1 lid 1, an employee who on December 31, 2009, is: 50 years old receives an annual PBL of 102 hours from the first of the month in which they turn 55; 51 years old receives an annual PBL of 122 hours from the first of the month in which they turn 55; 52 years old receives an annual PBL of 142 hours from the first of the month in which they turn 55; 53 years old receives an annual PBL of 157 hours from the first of the month in which they turn 55; 54 years old receives an annual PBL of 172 hours from the first of the month in which they turn 55; 55 years old but not yet 65 receives an annual PBL of 206 hours."

To claim this deposit, the employee, after a call from the employer, must report to the employer before December 1, 2009, to verify the service years criterion."

The PBL hours remaining on December 31, 2022, will be converted to Balansverlof (see Chapter 8A as of January 1, 2023). The indexation period for PBL hours for which the five-year indexation period has not yet expired on December 31, 2022, will be extended by five years."

    [
      "Balansverlof (Article 8A:1) (applies from January 1, 2023): The employee receives, in addition to statutory vacation hours, 57 hours of Balansverlof per calendar year on a full-time basis. On December 1, 2023, all employees employed on December 1, 2023, will receive a one-time extra 8 hours of Balansverlof, pro-rata to their contractual working hours and the duration of their employment in 2023. The Balansverlof is accrued monthly. The employee accrues Balansverlof for each calendar year in which they were entitled to salary for the full agreed working hours, unless otherwise agreed in this CAO. An employee who was entitled to salary for part of the calendar year accrues Balansverlof pro-rata for that part. The Balansverlof (including rights based on transition rights) can be taken by the employee entirely or partially as leave, entirely or partially saved in time, or partially paid out. The employee makes their choice known in time, prior to the calendar year."
    ],
    [
      "Balansverlof principles (Article 8A:2): The employer facilitates the sustainable employability of the employee with the Balansverlof. The employee gains more control (autonomy and responsibility) over their own work/life balance. The employee can use the Balansverlof, in consultation with the employer, for example, for: (timely) disengagement from work; a period of not working or working less; career orientation and personal development; personal special and/or unforeseen situations that impact the work-life balance. The employee makes an annual plan for the use of Balansverlof to promote their sustainable employability, which is discussed in the annual review. The employer manages the administration of the Balansverlof for time-saving purposes. The employee receives an annual overview of accrued Balansverlof from the employer. Balansverlof is accrued for the first six months of sickness/disability. Thereafter, no further accrual occurs for the hours of disability. Periods are aggregated if they follow each other with an interruption of less than one month. The employee can request to purchase a maximum of 7 extra days of Balansverlof (50.4 hours, pro-rata for part-timers). Extra Balansverlof hours can only be purchased for use in the calendar year of purchase and are not eligible for sale. A rejection of the request must be motivated in writing. Extra hours worked based on Article 6:11 serve as a saving source. The employer can designate other additional saving sources with the consent of the works council/personnel representation. The employee can save leave up to the maximum fiscally allowed (100 times the then-current contractual working hours per week). Exceeding the maximum fiscally allowed amount is prevented by timely taking Balansverlof and not by paying out Balansverlof, other than as specified below. The employee has the annual option to have 50% of the Balansverlof right added in that year paid out. In special situations, employer and employee can agree otherwise. The employer agrees to the request unless compelling business interests prevent it; in such a case, the payout of Balansverlof takes place at a later determined time, but no later than 6 months after the employee's request. Upon termination of employment/death, the remaining Balansverlof is paid out. The Balansverlof can, at the employee's request upon termination of employment, be transferred to another employer falling under the scope of the CAO Gehandicaptenzorg, if and to the extent legally and fiscally permitted. The transfer must concern at least 50 hours. Balansverlof accrued over the worked hours of a flexible worker and overtime of a part-timer is paid out as a surcharge on the hourly wage, unless employer and employee agree otherwise."
    ],
    [
      "Taking Balansverlof (Article 8A:3): Balansverlof is taken at the employee's discretion, in accordance with the consultation mentioned in Article 8A:2 lid 2, applying the following: leave is taken on a first-in, first-out basis; for substantial Balansverlof leave, the employee submits a written request to the employer at least four months before the start of the leave; for non-substantial Balansverlof leave, the employee submits their written request within a reasonable period; the employer grants the request for leave unless compelling business interests prevent it, requiring the employee's interest to yield based on standards of reasonableness and fairness. In case of concurrent Balansverlof leave and sickness, the Balansverlof leave is suspended for the duration of the sickness. The employer and employee make a new agreement on the end date of the leave. Upon the employer's request, the employee provides a doctor's statement. If the employee takes Balansverlof for career orientation and/or personal development, these hours are increased by 20%."
    ],
    [
      "Saving Balansverlof with employer (time saving) (Article 8A:4): The Balansverlof can be entirely or partially saved in time with the employer. Balansverlof from any calendar year can be saved value-protected for a maximum of ten years at the value of the current hourly wage. If the employee has not taken the Balansverlof (partially) within the ten-year period, the Balansverlof is paid out at the end of the calendar year after the indexation period. Deviating from lid 3, the employer and employee can agree otherwise, if the employee wishes, based on a motivated proposal from the employee in the context of their sustainable employability."
    ],

The amount of the transition right includes the Balansverlof right based on Article 8A:1."

  "termination_information": [
    [
      "End of employment contract (Article 2:5): The employment contract ends: by mutual consent; when the employee has reached the AOW-eligible age; by operation of law upon the expiry of the term or the termination of the activities for which the agreement was entered into; in the case of an indefinite-term agreement, by termination with due observance of Article 2:6; by termination for urgent reasons, as referred to in Articles 7:678 and 7:679 BW; by dissolution by the subdistrict court pursuant to Article 7:671b or 7:671c BW; immediately, by unilateral termination by the employer or employee during a written agreed probation period as referred to in Article 2:1 lid 2 sub f; by operation of law in case of the employee's death; on the first day of the following calendar month, if the employee who wishes to terminate the employment contract due to childbirth has notified the employer in writing no later than 10 calendar days after the birth; at the time when the right to the bridging benefit under the pension scheme of the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn is exercised, unless the employee and employer agree otherwise in writing. For this purpose, the employer and employee consult no later than three months before the date on which the employee wishes to use the bridging benefit. The employee must notify the employer in time that they will be using the bridging benefit. If the provision in lid 1 sub b applies, the agreement ends on the day preceding the day on which the employee reaches the AOW-eligible age, unless a different end date has been agreed upon in consultation between the employer and employee prior to this date."
    ],
    [
      "Notice of termination (Article 2:6): In case of termination as referred to in Article 2:5 lid 1 d or, if a provision for interim termination is included in a fixed-term employment contract, the statutory provisions regarding notice periods apply, unless expressly stated otherwise in this CAO. If the employer terminates the employment contract as referred to in sub a, this must be done in writing. Pursuant to Article 7:672 BW, if the notice period for the employee is extended, the notice period for the employee and the employer is equal if the notice period is not longer than 3 months. For a notice period longer than 3 months, the employer's notice period is twice as long as the employee's notice period. The effective date of dismissal as referred to in this article is the first of the calendar month."
    ],
    [
      "Suspension (Op non-actiefstelling) (Article 2:7): The employer can suspend the employee for a period of up to 3 weeks if, in the employer's judgment, the progress of work – for whatever reason – is seriously hindered. The term observed must be as limited as possible, but as long as necessary to achieve solutions. If this is not possible after the 3-week period, the term can, in principle, be extended by a maximum of 6 weeks after consultation with the employee or their advisor. If causes beyond the employer's control necessitate it, the employer can extend this term for a period longer than 6 weeks with justification. After a dismissal procedure has been initiated, the employer can extend the suspension for a term to be determined by them, until the time the employment contract ends or the dismissal procedure has ended. The decision to suspend, as well as the decision to extend it, must be communicated by the employer to the employee in writing as soon as possible, stating the reasons why this measure is required. Suspension always occurs with retention of all other rights arising from the employment contract/CAO. The employer is obliged, during the period of suspension, to promote the employee's ability to resume their work. After the expiry of the suspension period, the employee is entitled to resume their work. Suspension cannot be used as a disciplinary measure."
    ],
    [
      "Suspension (Schorsing) (Article 2:8): The employer can suspend the employee for a maximum of one week with retention of salary, for such serious reasons that continuation of the employee's work is no longer deemed responsible by the employer. The suspension must be communicated or confirmed to the employee immediately, with justification, by registered letter. The employee is given the opportunity to account for themselves to the employer within 4 days after the date of the registered letter (excluding Saturdays, Sundays, and public holidays). They can be assisted by counsel. The employer can extend the suspension once by a maximum of one week. The employer cannot simultaneously extend the suspension when first imposing it. If a dismissal procedure has been initiated by the employer with the UWV or a request for dissolution of the employment contract has been submitted to the court or arbitration tribunal, the suspension can be extended until the effective date of dismissal or termination of the employment contract. In case of extension of the suspension, the employee retains their salary. The employer is authorized to deny the employee access to the institution's buildings and grounds during the suspension period, insofar as it does not relate to the employee's living space. If it appears that the employee was demonstrably wrongly suspended by the employer, the employer will, at the employee's request, publicly rehabilitate them and compensate them for demonstrable damage."
    ],
    [
      "Compensation for damage to employee (Article 3:5): Without prejudice to the employer's legal liability for damage suffered by the employee in the performance of their duties, the following provisions apply. Material damage caused to the employee by a client and which could not reasonably have been prevented, is compensated by the employer based on replacement value, in accordance with the provisions below. Material damage in this context exclusively means: damage to the employee's goods; damage due to injury, insofar as it concerns recovery costs and costs due to permanent disability, all for a maximum of 24 months, calculated from the day the damage-causing event occurred. The damages mentioned under a and b are compensated together up to a maximum of €2269 per event. As of July 1, 2022, the maximum compensation amount will be increased to €3000 per event. To be eligible for compensation, the employee must demonstrate that: a client caused the damage; they cannot be compensated in any other way for this matter; the damage was caused in the performance of their function; they have otherwise adequately insured themselves against risks, where this is customary. By compensating the employee based on this article, the employer assumes the employee's rights, up to a maximum of €2269 (as of July 1, 2022, €3000), against the person who caused the damage. The employee is expected to wear clothing or carry goods that are appropriate for the employee's function and the characteristics and risks of the institution's client population."
    ],
    [
      "Career Reduction Facility (Afbouw Carrière) (Article 8A:10) (applies from July 1, 2022): The 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme allows employees to reduce their working hours for a maximum period of 3 years prior to reaching the AOW-eligible age, in order to maintain mental and physical balance and remain valuable to the organization and colleagues. The scheme is based on joint investment by employer and employee. The scheme is valid for the duration of the CAO. Employees can enter the scheme until January 31, 2024. If the 'Even ietsje minder' scheme is not yet in effect by February 1, 2024, employees can enter the 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme until the 'Even ietsje minder' scheme takes effect, but no later than December 31, 2025. The scheme remains applicable to employees who entered by December 31, 2025."

      "Conditions for Career Reduction Facility (Article 8A:11): An employee who meets the requirements can use the 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme for a maximum of 3 years before reaching the AOW-eligible age. Conditions for participation are: The employee must have an indefinite-term employment contract of at least 24 hours per week at the desired start date, and have been continuously employed by the employer for at least 10 years in a direct client-related function, and 15 years in intellectually disabled care in a direct client-related function (guiding, treating, caring, and nursing). Participation must not result in the employee working less than 50% of their original contract size when combined with existing other schemes or instruments from the organization's HR policy. The employee must not already have reduced deployability due to a reintegration plan for illness or based on a WAO or WIA decision from the UWV. Prior to the 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme, the employee must take any saved PBL/Balansverlof in time, unless the employer and employee agree otherwise."

      "Participation in Career Reduction Facility (Article 8A:12): Upon participation in the 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme: The contractual working hours are reduced by 25% (becoming 75% of the original employment contract). The employee receives a salary supplement so that they earn 85% of their original salary. Pension accrual will be based on the new pensionable salary (85%). The employee can apply for voluntary continuation with PFZW, in which case pension accrual remains based on the pensionable salary corresponding to the original working hours (100%). The distribution of pension premiums between employer and employee remains 50/50% in both cases. The employer will support the employee where possible in applying for voluntary continuation. During participation in the 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme, the employee accrues PBL/Balansverlof based on the adjusted contractual working hours (Article 8A:12 lid 1 sub a). The accrual of PBL/Balansverlof transition rights based on Article 8A:7 ceases during participation in the 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme. The employee is not permitted to perform other paid work during participation in the 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme, other than what was already being performed prior to participation, unless the employer and employee agree otherwise in specific situations. Taking PBL/Balansverlof during the 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme is only permitted if and to the extent that the 'Afbouw Carrière' scheme is not classified as an RVU (early retirement scheme)."

      "Guarantee right (Article 15:2): The guarantee right is a supplement to the transition payment in cases where the maximum sum of benefits from the waiting allowance scheme from CAO 2014-2015 would have been higher than the statutory transition payment. This means the employee receives a supplement to the transition payment under the conditions below. The guarantee right applies to employees with an indefinite-term employment contract who are dismissed exclusively due to: reduction/cessation of work or reorganization, OR incompetence/unsuitability for the function to be performed in the institution, not attributable to their fault or doing, resulting from developments in the organization or concerning their function or profession, which are external to the employee (where incompetence/unsuitability does not mean disability). For the calculation of the guarantee right, the following are included: consecutive full years spent with the employer, AND consecutive full months with other employers falling under the scope of the CAO Gehandicaptenzorg immediately preceding the employment with the current employer (these months are rounded to full years). Service years with other employers are not included if a benefit based on the guarantee right has already been determined for them. The employee is obliged to inform the employer about previous employment with other employers falling under the scope of the CAO Gehandicaptenzorg and whether these were included in a previous calculation of the guarantee right. The guarantee right is determined using the 'guarantee right' table in Article 15:3. If the transition payment is lower than the amount determined by the table in Article 15:3, the employee receives a supplement to the transition payment equal to the difference. The supplement, with due observance of this article, also applies to employees with an indefinite-term employment contract who are dismissed and have been employed for less than 24 months at the end of the employment contract. The guarantee right, being the supplement to the transition payment, cannot exceed the maximum expected income loss until the AOW-eligible age. The transition payment increased by the guarantee right cannot exceed the maximum specified in Article 7:673 BW. The supplement based on the guarantee right is a one-time payment, unless the employer and employee agree otherwise."

  "overtime_information": [
    [
      "Research on special services (Page 8): CAO parties will investigate the compensation, application, coherence, and possible discrepancies of special services such as extra shifts, shifted shifts, overtime, and irregular services, from the perspective of valuing flexible deployment of employees."
    ],
    [
      "Working and rest times (Article 6:6): The employer can assign irregular shifts (weekend, holidays, evening and night shifts), on-call duties, standby duties, consultation duties, presence duties, and sleep duties, and assign overtime to the employee (See also Article 6:10 and Chapter 7)."
    ],
    [
      "Shifted shifts (Verschoven diensten) (Article 6:8): If, due to special circumstances of an incidental nature, the interest of the service requires it, the employer can, after consultation with the employee, make changes to an already established working time schedule as referred to in Article 6:6 lid 1. The employee receives compensation for this based on the provisions of Article 7:12 of the CAO. A shifted shift in the sense of this article only occurs if a number of consecutive hours, during which the employee was scheduled to work according to the established working time schedule or roster, is moved by the employer to any other time when the employee was not scheduled to work according to the established working time schedule or roster."
    ],
    [
      "Working hours for pregnant employees and older employees (Article 6:10): In deviation from Article 6:6 lid 2 of this CAO, a pregnant employee cannot be obliged to perform overtime, irregular shifts, on-call duties, standby duties, consultation duties, presence duties, or sleep duties. In deviation from Article 6:6 lid 2, an employee aged 57 and older cannot be obliged to perform night shifts and sleep duties. An employee aged 57 or older who chooses to be structurally scheduled for night shifts works 40 hours less per year (pro-rata to the employment size)."
    ],
    [
      "Working extra hours above contractual basic working hours (Article 6:11): The employee can submit a written request to the employer (annually) to work extra hours above the contractually agreed hours for a period to be agreed upon. A rejection of the request must be motivated in writing. The employer and the employee make written agreements about the period for saving and taking the extra worked hours. If the employee, partly due to the extra worked hours, saves more leave than is fiscally permitted, they are liable for income tax and premiums on the excess leave. The leave mentioned in the previous sentence includes any increase in the number of hours by 20% based on Article 10:7 lid 4 of this CAO (applicable until December 31, 2022)."
    ],
    [
      "Definitions (Article 7:1):",
      "Overtime (Overwerk): Work performed incidentally beyond the working hours established in the work and rest time pattern, where the exceeding of working hours is measured on a semi-annual basis. Overtime does not include shifted shifts.",
      "On-call duty (Bereikbaarheidsdienst): A continuous period of a maximum of 24 hours during which the employee, if necessary in addition to performing the agreed work, is obliged to be available to perform the agreed work as soon as possible upon call.",
      "Standby duty (Consignatiedienst): A period between two consecutive shifts or during a break, during which the employee is exclusively obliged to be available to perform the agreed work as soon as possible upon call in unforeseen circumstances.",
      "Irregular shift (Onregelmatige dienst): Work performed according to a roster during the hours specified in Article 7:11. Irregular shift also includes work performed by a part-time employee not according to a roster during the hours specified in Article 7:11, exceeding their contractually agreed number of hours, as long as it does not exceed 1878 hours per year.",
      "Sleep shift (Slaapdienst): A continuous period of at least 6 and a maximum of 8 hours between 23:00 and 09:00, during which the employee is present and available in the institution for performing necessary and unforeseen work, but primarily rests.",
      "Consultation duty (Consultatiedienst): The circumstance that an employee must be reachable by telephone for consultation outside scheduled working hours."
    ],
    [
      "Order for overtime (Article 7:2): Compensation for overtime is granted if the employee has received an order for overtime, or could reasonably assume that they would have received an order for overtime. In such a case, the employer retrospectively determines the necessity of overtime."
    ],
    [
      "Determining number of overtime hours (Article 7:3): If overtime is performed for a period of half an hour or less preceding or immediately following the working hours established by the work schedule, this period is not eligible for compensation. If overtime is performed for a period longer than half an hour, this period is rounded to a full hour. If overtime is performed for a period longer than one hour, this period is rounded up to half or full hours, respectively."
    ],
    [
      "Compensation for full-time employees (Article 7:4): Compensation for overtime is provided – unless lid 3 of this article states otherwise – in the form of free time, equal to the number of hours the overtime lasted, and additionally in the form of a monetary payment as referred to in lid 2 of this article. The monetary payment mentioned under 1 consists of a percentage of the hourly wage, specifically: 25% for overtime performed between 06:00 and 22:00 from Monday to Friday, with the understanding that in a period of seven days, the number of hours to be compensated in this way is a maximum of 5; the remaining hours are compensated with 50%; 50% for overtime performed between 22:00 and 06:00 from Monday to Friday; 75% for overtime performed on Saturday until 18:00 and on free days; 100% for overtime performed on Saturday from 18:00, on Sundays and public holidays between 00:00 and 24:00, and on December 24 and 31 between 18:00 and 24:00. For the purpose of this article, 'free days' are defined as days, not being Sunday or a public holiday, on which the employee would not have to work according to their work and rest time pattern. The right to overtime compensation as mentioned under lid 1 is granted in the following cases: if the employee's salary does not exceed IP-number 48 of the classification table; if the employee's salary exceeds IP-number 48 of the classification table and insofar as the number of hours worked above the working hours included in the work and rest time pattern amounts to more than an average of 6 per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies. Deviating from the provisions in lid 3 under a and b, CAO parties can establish a different hourly norm for overtime for certain categories of employees."
    ],

      "Compensation for part-time employees (Article 7:5): Overtime compensation consists of the employee's applicable hourly wage, if and insofar as the number of overtime hours, measured over a six-month period, does not exceed the difference between the employee's applicable working hours and the working hours for full-time employment. This compensation includes the accrual of holiday pay and year-end bonus, as well as the accrual of vacation hours and PBL/Balansverlof hours. Additionally, compensation as mentioned in Article 7:4 is granted if and insofar as the number of overtime hours, measured over a six-month period, exceeds the difference between the employee's applicable working hours and the working hours for full-time employment."

      "Wage: The agreed financial compensation for performing the agreed work. For every employee, wage includes: the period or basic (hourly) wage; the holiday allowance (it is not intended that holiday allowance is paid over holiday allowance); diploma allowance; commission related to individual performance; fixed allowances (this also includes the allowance for working specific hours, if the employee regularly works these hours)."

    [
      "Determination of guaranteed wage: The wage paid to an employee is linked to a job group in which the employee's function is classified. The employee receives a wage that is at least equal to the amount determined for their age or for their job years according to the wage scales (see also Appendix 1b of this CAO). The amounts included therein are gross and are stated both on the basis of an hour worked and per month with an average working time of 38 hours per week, and for Wonen 37 hours per week."

    [
      "Wage adjustment: On 1 January 2023, the scale wages and the wages of employees who earn no more than the old maximum of their scale will be increased by 7.46%. On 1 January 2023, the wages of employees who earn more than the old maximum of their scale, but less than the new maximum, will be increased to the new maximum of their scale. Wage increases given after 1 July 2022 in anticipation of a CAO increase (excluding one-off payments, bonuses, and commission) may be offset against the wage increase of 1 January 2023. Employees who do not receive a CAO wage increase on 1 January 2023, earn more than the new maximum of their scale, but not more than €4400, will receive a one-off payment of 2.5% over the wage from June 2022 to May 2023 on 1 June 2023. The total wage is the wage over which holiday allowance is calculated. For employees who started employment after 1 July 2022, the one-off payment will be calculated over the wage from the moment of commencement of employment up to and including May 2023. Wage increases given from 2 July 2022 to 30 June 2023 (excluding one-off payments, bonuses, and commission) may be offset against the one-off payment. On 1 July 2023, the scale wages and the wages of employees who earn no more than the old maximum of their scale will be increased by the percentage by which the Wettelijk Minimumloon is increased on that date. On 1 July 2023, the wages of employees who earn more than the old maximum of their scale, but less than the new maximum, will be increased to the new maximum of their scale. Wage increases given after 1 January 2023 in anticipation of a CAO increase (excluding one-off payments, bonuses, and commission) may be offset against this."

If the system of performance-based pay is applied within a company, the experience year is replaced by the performance increase. A company can choose to grant the performance increase at a fixed time of the year – this fixed time then applies to all employees – which means that the increase date does not have to coincide with the employee reaching the experience year. In that case, every employee with at least 6 months of experience in a function must be eligible for the performance increase."

    [
      "Continued wage payment during incapacity for work: In deviation from art. 7:629 BW, the employee is entitled to continued wage payment of 100% of the gross wage during the first 26 weeks of incapacity for work. In the subsequent period of 26 weeks (week 27 to 52), 90% of the gross wage, but at least the WML, is paid. In the period of 26 weeks thereafter (week 53 to 78), 80% of the gross wage is paid. In the period of 26 weeks thereafter (week 79 to 104), the statutory wage payment obligation of 70% of the gross wage applies. With regard to employees who have reached the AOW-eligible age, the statutory wage payment obligation for AOW-eligible individuals applies. Per sickness case, 1 waiting day can be deducted. Periods of sickness are aggregated if they follow each other with an interruption of less than four weeks. The employer does not have to pay wages for a waiting day. The employer is allowed to offset the waiting day with vacation hours that have been granted to the employee above the statutory minimum, with the wage, or with compensation days/hours, unless otherwise agreed with the works council. A maximum of four waiting days per year can be offset with vacation hours or deducted from the wage. If the Arboarts/Bedrijfsarts (being a doctor from the company's Arbodienst) has diagnosed a chronic illness in the employee, the employer will be extremely reluctant to deduct waiting days. For the determination of the number of paid sick leave hours per day and the number of hours for the waiting day, the number of hours the employee would have worked according to their roster is used. If the employee does not work in a shop and the working hours cannot be determined in advance due to external circumstances (e.g., for drivers), the number of contract hours per week can be used instead of the rostered hours. If no roster has been established, the number of hours the employee has worked per day on average in the last 13 weeks preceding the incapacity for work is used. If that reference period is not representative in the opinion of the employer, the last 52 weeks preceding the day of sickness are used. Sickness cases that follow each other within a period of 4 weeks are counted as one sickness case, regardless of the cause of the sickness. Any benefits under social insurance laws that are payable via the employer will be deducted from the wage payments. The continued wage payments mentioned in this article end at the termination of the employment contract of the employee concerned. If the employer is obliged to continue paying wages during incapacity for work, the employee must transfer their rights against third parties (including those of the UWV) due to wage loss from incapacity for work to the employer. If the incapacity for work lasts continuously for more than one month, the employer is not obliged to pay work-related expense reimbursements after that month."

The wage for the full month in which the employee's death occurred will be paid out. In addition, two more monthly wages will be paid out."

      "Implementation agreements and wage structure system - Performance-based pay: If the actual wage of the employee is within the basic wage structure: Introduction at the next higher step on a date to be determined, which may deviate from the introduction date of the new wage structure. CAO increases apply to the wage table. Only an employee who receives an insufficient assessment again after an insufficient assessment is not entitled to the CAO increase. A very good or excellent performing employee is entitled to an increase of 1 or 2 steps. A sufficiently performing employee is entitled to a step increase. A moderate or insufficiently performing employee is not entitled to a step increase. There is flanking policy to improve performance. If the actual wage of the employee is within the extra wage structure: Introduction at the actual wage. A very good or excellent performing employee is entitled to a CAO increase and an increase of 1 or 2 steps of 2%. A sufficiently performing employee is entitled to a CAO increase, but not a step increase. A moderate or insufficiently performing employee is not entitled to a CAO increase and also not a step increase. There is flanking policy to improve performance. If the actual wage of the employee is above the highest step of their scale in the extra wage structure: No standard CAO increases apply. Individual agreements between employer and employee. Employees younger than 21 years are not paid performance-based, but only receive their age increase."

Part A applies to employees who were employed on March 31, 2022, under a contract catering CAO (indefinite or fixed-term until expiry). Part B applies to employees hired on or after April 1, 2022, or with a subsequent fixed-term contract on or after April 1, 2022; employees under the Horeca CAO on March 31, 2022, whose employer switches to the contract catering CAO; employees who voluntarily switch from Part A to Part B (possible on July 1, 2022, January 1, 2023, July 1, 2023, January 1, 2024, with two months' notice); employees who, after March 31, 2022, agree to perform work not previously covered by the contract catering CAO; employees who, due to reduced hours at their current location, agree to perform new work at another location to retain their contract hours. Employees switching from Part A to Part B will have all their contract hours (pro rata to 38 hours per week) transferred to Part B, and all rights from Part A will cease."
    ],
    [
      "Minimum CAO (Algemeen deel, Article 10): This CAO has a minimum character, meaning at least the provisions contained herein must be applied."

If none of the parties terminate the agreement by registered letter at least three months before its end, it is deemed extended for the same period, but for a maximum of one year."

  "leave_information": [
    [
      "Vacation Rights (Deel A, Article 51.1): Vacation rights consist of vacation days and holiday allowance."
    ],
    [
      "Proportional Application (Deel A, Article 51.2): Vacation rights apply proportionally to regional and part-time employees based on actual hours worked, with a maximum of 40 hours per week."
    ],
    [
      "Wage during Vacation (Deel A, Article 51.4): The employee retains the right to wage as defined in Article 7:639 of the Civil Code during vacation."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation Year (Deel A, Article 52.1): The vacation year runs from January 1 to January 1. The number of vacation days and vacation rights are calculated over this period."
    ],
    [
      "Number of Vacation Days (Deel A, Article 52.2a): Employees aged 18 and older who have been employed for the entire year are entitled to at least 25 days per year, of which at least three weeks are consecutive and the remainder are leave days. Employees younger than 18 are entitled to 28 vacation days per year. The total number of vacation days includes statutory and extra-statutory vacation days. The statutory entitlement to vacation is four times the agreed weekly working hours per vacation year."
    ],
    [
      "Saving Extra-Statutory Vacation Days (Deel A, Article 52.2b): Employees have the right to save extra-statutory vacation days accrued over a vacation year, subject to the provisions regarding expiry."
    ],
    [
      "Proportional Vacation Accrual (Deel A, Article 52.2c): Employees not employed for the entire vacation year are entitled to 1/12 of the vacation for each month of employment, with an employment period of 15 days or more counting as a full month, and less than 15 days not being counted."
    ],
    [
      "Consecutive Vacation (Deel A, Article 52.4): Consecutive vacation, if desired by the employee and if work permits, is granted in the months of April through September."
    ],
    [
      "Changing Vacation Period (Deel A, Article 52.5): The employer can, for weighty reasons and after consultation with the employee, change the fixed period of consecutive vacation. The employer compensates the employee for any demonstrable damage incurred due to this change."
    ],
    [
      "Special Vacation Days (Deel A, Article 52.6): Hindu employees have the right to take a vacation day on Diwali. Islamic employees have this right at the end of Ramadan and on Eid Curban, respectively."
    ],
    [
      "Expiry of Vacation Days (Deel A, Article 53.1): Statutory vacation days expire six months after the last day of the vacation year in which the entitlement was acquired. Extra-statutory vacation days expire five years after the last day of the vacation year in which the entitlement was acquired."
    ],
    [
      "Use of Expired Statutory Vacation Days (Deel A, Article 53.2): If statutory vacation days have not been taken due to incapacity for work or operational reasons not attributable to the employee, and a positive balance of statutory vacation days remains at the end of the expiry period, these can be used for training days or for funding training, up to a maximum of 10 days. These days can be utilized in a tax-friendly manner if they concern work-related training and the employer and employee have agreed in advance that this positive balance can be used for those trainings."
    ],
    [
      "Employer Designates Vacation Days (Deel A, Article 53.3): If the employee has not taken their statutory vacation days by May 31 following the end of the vacation year in which they were earned, the employer sets the dates for these vacation days."
    ],
    [
      "Holiday Allowance Calculation (Deel A, Article 54.1): The holiday allowance is 8% of the wage or sickness benefit (including supplements) earned by the employee from June 1 to May 31 of the current year. Incidental allowances, gratuities, and benefits in kind are not included. Shift allowance (Article 44) and irregular hours allowance (Article 39) are included if they are fixed. The sum of the wage and holiday allowance must be at least 108% of the minimum wage."
    ],
    [
      "Holiday Allowance Payment (Deel A, Article 54.2): The employer is obliged to pay the holiday allowance by May of that year."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation Rights upon Termination/Death (Deel A, Article 55.1): Upon termination of employment, the employer must pay out accrued vacation rights on the day of termination. The employer has the right to offset any overpaid vacation rights with the wage."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation Rights upon Death (Deel A, Article 55.2): If an employee dies, any unused vacation rights are paid to the next of kin."
    ],
    [
      "Special Leave (Deel A, Article 58.2): Paid leave is granted for: Death of spouse/life partner/child (day of death to funeral/cremation, min 4 days); Death of parent/parent-in-law (2 days); Burial/cremation of blood/affinity relatives up to grandparents (not spouse/parents/children), sibling, brother/sister-in-law (1 day); Employee's marriage/registered partnership (2 days); Marriage/registered partnership of child/foster child/grandchild, sibling, brother/sister-in-law, parent/parent-in-law (1 day, if attended); 25th wedding anniversary of employee, parents, parents-in-law (1 day, if 1 year continuous service); 40th, 50th, 60th wedding anniversary of parents, parents-in-law, grandparents (1 day, if 1 year continuous service); 40th wedding anniversary of employee (1 day); First Holy Communion, baptism, Berith mila, confirmation, Bar mitzvah of child/foster child/grandchild (1 day, if attended); Attending national meetings of signatory unions as official delegate, or union training/education courses (max 12 days/year, if work allows, timely consultation); Employee relocation (max 1 day/year); Fulfilling legal/government obligations without financial compensation (reasonable time, max 1 day); Doctor/dentist/specialist visits (reasonable time, if cannot be outside working hours)."
    ],
    [
      "Maternity and Childbirth Leave (Deel A, Article 68.1): The total duration of maternity and childbirth leave is the statutory period of 16 weeks."
    ],
    [
      "Flexible Maternity Leave (Deel A, Article 68.2): Maternity leave can be taken flexibly, starting no earlier than 6 weeks and no later than 4 weeks before the expected delivery date. Employees pregnant with multiple births are entitled to four additional weeks of maternity leave."
    ],
    [
      "Flexible Childbirth Leave (Deel A, Article 68.3): Childbirth leave can be taken flexibly from the sixth week after delivery over a period of up to 30 weeks."
    ],
    [
      "Birth Leave for Partner (Deel A, Article 68.4): The mother's partner is entitled to birth leave with pay, equal to the number of working hours per week, after the birth."
    ],
    [
      "Parental Leave (Deel A, Article 69.1): The employee is entitled to parental leave in accordance with the statutory regulations laid down in the Wet Arbeid en Zorg."
    ],
    [
      "Calamity Leave (Deel A, Article 70.2): The employee is entitled to 1 day of paid leave per year in case of a calamity."
    ],
    [
      "Extended Unpaid Calamity Leave (Deel A, Article 70.3): A longer duration of leave, without pay, can be agreed upon between employer and employee, with a maximum of 9 days per year."
    ],
    [
      "Care Leave (Deel A, Article 71.2): The duration of care leave cannot exceed 30 days per year, of which 8 days are paid: 95% for the first 3 days and 70% for the remaining 5 days."
    ],
    [
      "Care Leave in Hours (Deel A, Article 71.3): Care leave can be taken in hours, provided it is operationally plannable, preferably at the beginning or end of the shift."
    ],
    [
      "Roster-Free Days (Deel A, Article 22.1): Employees are granted 13 roster-free days per year with retention of wage. For employees working in a 5-shift roster, Article 16, paragraph 2 applies."
    ],
    [
      "Scheduling Roster-Free Days (Deel A, Article 22.2a): A minimum of 6 roster-free days for any year are scheduled in November of the preceding year, in consultation with the employee."
    ],
    [
      "Flexible Roster-Free Days (Deel A, Article 22.2b): Four roster-free days are reserved for flexible working hours (Article 17A). If a roster-free day is not needed to offset 'minus hours' in a calendar quarter, the employer may schedule this roster-free day in the subsequent calendar quarter, within 14 days after the end of the quarter."
    ],
    [
      "Collective Roster-Free Days (Deel A, Article 22.4): The employer can designate 3 roster-free days collectively per location annually, to be announced before December 1 of any year."
    ],
    [
      "Roster-Free Days and Sickness (Deel A, Article 22.5a): If an employee is incapacitated for work on scheduled roster-free days, these days are not forfeited. Replacement roster-free time will be determined in consultation after recovery."
    ],
    [
      "Expiry of Roster-Free Days (Deel A, Article 22.5b): Roster-free days not taken due to incapacity for work or operational reasons not attributable to the employee expire after 5 years."
    ],
    [
      "No Roster-Free Days during Prolonged Sickness (Deel A, Article 22.5c): If incapacity for work lasts longer than 6 months, no roster-free days are granted for the period of incapacity after these 6 months. If the 6-month period is interrupted by a period of work capacity shorter than 4 weeks, the 6-month period of incapacity for work continues as a single period."
    ],
    [
      "Additional Working Hour Reduction for Older Employees (Deel A, Article 22.6): Employees born before January 1, 1961, are entitled to further working hour reduction from the month they turn 55: 55 years (36 hours/year), 56 years (48 hours/year), 57 years (60 hours/year), 58 years (72 hours/year), 59 to 66 years (84 hours/year)."
    ],
    [
      "Additional Working Hour Reduction for Older Employees (Deel A, Article 22.7): Employees born on or after January 1, 1961, are entitled to further working hour reduction from the month they turn 57: 57 years (36 hours/year), 58 years (48 hours/year), 59 years (60 hours/year), 60 years (72 hours/year), 61 to 66 years (84 hours/year)."
    ],
    [
      "Exchange of Roster-Free Days for Wage (Deel A, Article 56.1): Employees can voluntarily exchange roster-free days, up to a maximum of 9 days per year, for wage. The number of days depends on the number of days the employer designates collectively per location. The employee can choose to have this wage paid out in a lump sum or monthly. The sale of roster-free days, including extra roster-free time for older employees (Article 22.6), can be used to fully or partially compensate the employee's premium for the VUT Transition Scheme."
    ],
    [
      "Value of Roster-Free Day (Deel A, Article 56.2): The value of a full roster-free day is set at 0.45% of the wage (gross monthly wage multiplied by 12 plus holiday allowance)."
    ],
    [
      "Buying Roster-Free Days (Deel A, Article 56.3): Employees can also buy days annually, with a value of 0.45% of the wage."
    ],
    [
      "Seniors Exchanging Wage for Roster-Free Days (Deel A, Article 56.4): Employees aged 55 and older can voluntarily exchange wage for roster-free days annually. The value of such a day is set at 0.3% of the wage. If this scheme is used, the schemes mentioned in paragraphs 1 to 3 cannot be used."
    ],
    [
      "Choice for Buying/Selling Roster-Free Days (Deel A, Article 56.5): The choice for buying or selling roster-free days must be communicated by the employee to the employer before December 1."
    ],
    [
      "Saving Hours Scheme (Deel A.D, Article 2.1): For locations with a fixed company closure, if the number of vacation days an employee is entitled to is less than the number of days per year on which no work can be performed due to the closure, the employer and employee can agree on a saving hours scheme."
    ],
    [
      "Saving Hours Scheme Regulations (Deel A.D, Bijlage A.D1a): To achieve a stable income pattern over the year, employer and employee agree on a saving hours scheme. It applies if vacation days are less than closure days. Vacation days are taken during closure periods. Employer informs employee of closure periods by October 1 and the difference in hours to be saved. Recognized public holidays are included in the calculation of saving hours. Employer provides monthly overview. Accrual continues during sickness. If sick during school holiday/saving hours, saving hours are deducted. Additional/overtime hours are paid in the next pay period. Upon termination, saved hours are paid out. Employee authorizes employer to save hours by not paying out certain worked hours each pay period, with the number of hours to save per period agreed upon."
    ]

  "termination_information": [
    [
      "Probation Period (Deel A, Article 6.2): For contracts up to and including 6 months, no probation period. For contracts longer than 6 months up to 2 years, 1 month probation. For contracts of 2 years or longer, or indefinite, 2 months probation."
    ],
    [
      "Termination during Probation (Deel A, Article 6.3): During and at the end of the probation period, both the employer and the employee can terminate the employment contract without observing a notice period. This also applies if the employee is incapacitated for work during the probation period."
    ],
    [
      "Chain of Employment Contracts (Deel A, Article 7): If more than three fixed-term employment contracts have successively followed each other between the same parties within a maximum period of three years, with intervals of no more than six months, the last employment contract is considered to be for an indefinite period. Temporary agency work for the same activities, up to a maximum of three years, does not count towards this chain."
    ],
    [
      "End of Employment Contract (Deel A, Article 8.1): The provisions of the Civil Code apply to the termination of the employment contract."
    ],
    [
      "Fixed-Term Contract Expiry (Deel A, Article 8.2): Fixed-term contracts that have been extended for the first, second, or third time do not require notice (i.e., no prior permission from UWV is needed). For contracts of six months or longer, the employer must inform the employee in writing at least one month before the contract ends by operation of law whether it will be continued. For contracts shorter than six months, timely notice (one week) must be given by the party not wishing to continue the contract."
    ],
    [
      "Damages for Untimely Notice (Deel A, Article 8.3): If a party fails to comply with the notice obligation, the other party is entitled to compensation in accordance with statutory provisions."
    ],
    [
      "Termination at AOW-Eligible Age (Deel A, Article 8.4): The employment contract ends by operation of law, without requiring notice, on the day the employee reaches the AOW-eligible age, unless a later date has been agreed upon by the employer and employee. If a new employment contract is entered into after reaching the AOW-eligible age, the Wet werken na de AOW-gerechtigde leeftijd applies instead of Articles 6, 8, and 64 of this CAO."
    ],
    [
      "Dismissal Protection during Sickness (Deel A, Article 8.5): The employer cannot use permission for dismissal for a maximum of three months if the onset of incapacity for work due to a physical work accident occurs after the request for dismissal permission has been received by the UWV."
    ],
    [
      "Termination by New Employer (Deel A, Article 10.6): A new employer has the right to terminate the employment contract with an employee who has joined them due to a contract change, if the new employer has no work for the employee as a result of the (organization of) work after the contract change. For such a termination, the new employer will follow the appropriate procedure via UWV, unless the contract can be terminated in another legally valid way."
    ],
    [
      "Termination of Employment for 35-80% Disabled (Deel A, Article 67.1c): If reintegration efforts fail for partially disabled employees (between 35% and 80% incapacitated), the employment relationship can be terminated 12 calendar months after the start of the WIA, in compliance with legal provisions. This does not apply to the inflight catering sector for direct employees who are or become 35-80% disabled after January 1, 2018, or for other employees in this sector, for whom Article 12 of Part A.C applies."
    ],
    [
      "Termination of Employment for Structurally Partially Disabled Inflight Catering Direct Employees (Deel A.C, Article 12.5): For inflight catering direct employees who, after January 1, 2018, are or become structurally partially disabled and cannot be redeployed in suitable work because the 7% limit has been reached or due to individual specific circumstances, the employment relationship can be terminated in accordance with legal provisions."
    ],
    [
      "Social Plan (Deel A, Article 83): If measures mentioned in Article 82 (business termination, reorganization, etc.) have consequences for employees, the employer will draw up a social plan in consultation with the employee organizations. This plan will specify which employee interests will be particularly considered and what provisions will be made in connection therewith. If requested by the employee organizations, the UWV's insight regarding the employability of the affected employees will also be sought."
    ]
  ],
  "overtime_information": [
    [
      "Overtime Definition (Deel A, Article 1.13): Overtime is work, ordered by or on behalf of the employer, performed above the normal working hours per week or 9 hours per day. For employees younger than 18, overtime is work performed above the normal working hours per week or 8 hours per day."
    ],
    [
      "Obligation to Work Overtime (Deel A, Article 35): Overtime is mandatory insofar as business requirements, in the employer's judgment, make it necessary. For employees aged 50 or older, there is no obligation to work overtime. Overtime is permitted up to the maximum working hours."
    ],
    [
      "Maximum Working Hours (Deel A, Article 36.1): The employer organizes work such that youth employees work a maximum of 9 hours per shift, 45 hours per week, and an average of 40 hours per week in every period of 4 consecutive weeks."
    ],
    [
      "Maximum Working Hours (Deel A, Article 36.2): Employees aged 18 or older work a maximum of 48 hours per week."
    ],
    [
      "Overtime Allowance (Deel A, Article 37.1): Employees who have worked overtime are entitled to an overtime allowance as specified in the applicable overtime matrix. The right to the allowance applies from the moment the normal working time is exceeded by 15 minutes, retroactively to the start of these 15 minutes."
    ],
    [
      "Overtime Allowance for 4- and 5-Shift Work (Deel A, Article 37.2): For 4- and 5-shift work, a fixed overtime allowance of 75% applies."
    ],
    [
      "Overtime Exception (Deel A, Article 38): For employees with a wage level of salary group 7 or higher who are voluntarily affiliated with this CAO through their employer, the CAO provisions regarding overtime do not apply, with existing rights reserved."
    ],
    [
      "Irregular Hours Allowances (Deel A, Article 39.1): When an employee works irregular hours, they are entitled to financial compensation as specified in the applicable irregular hours matrix."
    ],
    [
      "Irregular Hours Exception (Deel A, Article 39.2): For employees with a wage level of salary group 7 or higher who are voluntarily affiliated with this CAO through their employer, the CAO provisions regarding irregular hours do not apply, with existing rights reserved."
    ],
    [
      "Anti-Accumulation of Overtime and Irregularity Allowance (Deel A, Article 40): There is no obligation for the employer to cumulatively apply the applicable overtime matrix and the applicable irregularity matrix. The employer must apply the most favorable regulation for the employee."
    ],
    [
      "Public Holiday Allowance (Deel A, Article 41): For work performed on public holidays as defined in the CAO, the percentages from the irregular hours matrix applicable to the employee apply, increased by a compensation of 100%. This 100% compensation will, however, be granted as free time as much as possible. The employer is obliged to grant this free time within a period of 2 weeks preceding or 6 weeks following the calendar month in which the public holiday falls."
    ],
    [
      "Retention of Irregular Hours Allowance (Deel A, Article 42): An employee who, at the employer's instruction, no longer works irregular hours, retains the right to irregular hours allowances for 1 month, provided these were fixed in their employment. Thereafter, the conditions of the new situation apply."
    ],
    [
      "Retention of Allowance for Continuous Shift Workers (Deel A, Article 43): An employee who, at the employer's instruction or for health reasons, no longer works in continuous shifts, retains their irregular hours allowance for as many months as they have enjoyed the relevant allowance in years, up to a maximum of 6 months. If the employee received the relevant allowance for part of a year, this is counted as a full year for the application of this scheme."
    ],
    [
      "Shift Work Allowances (Deel A, Article 44): For allowances for shift work (regardless of the shift roster), the irregular hours allowance matrix as applicable and included as an appendix to the respective sectors (special parts A to D) applies. An annual average can be used per payment period."
    ],
    [
      "Phasing-Out Scheme for Shift Workers (Deel A, Article 45): For employees who have worked in shift work for at least one continuous year and who, at the employer's instruction or for health reasons, can no longer work in shift work, resulting in adverse financial consequences, the following phasing-out scheme applies: a. for six salary periods, the allowance or income according to the shift work scheme; b. after this period, the difference between earnings during shift work and earnings in the new situation will be phased out by: 50% per year if the shift work scheme lasted less than three consecutive years; 25% per year if the shift work scheme lasted from three to five consecutive years; 15% per year if the shift work scheme lasted more than five consecutive years. c. If the employee worked in the shift work scheme for part of a year, this is counted as a full year for the application of this scheme. d. If the shift work scheme ends because the employee is 55 years or older, the terms under a. and b. are doubled."
    ],
    [
      "On-Call Allowance (Deel A, Article 50.5): An employee who is on-call at the employer's instruction is entitled to an on-call allowance of €0.68 per hour. The on-call compensation is at least 3 hours."

Necessary trainings are generally followed during working hours.
